# Environment flags — the audited catalog

> **A new `MEMRA_*` read needs a row here IN THE SAME COMMIT.** `tools/hooks/pre-push` runs
> `tools/check-flags.sh` on every push (+0.55 s) and refuses one that adds an uncovered name. That
> arm landed 2026-08-23 after main went red three times in one day on this exact rule — the census
> was correct, but its only enforcement was a CI job, so every violation was discovered *after*
> landing and the lanes queued behind it each paid a repair commit. The refusal message names the
> missing rows; the emergency escape and its logging are in CLAUDE.md beside the other hook arms.
>
> **There is no grandfather list to add a name to** — do not go looking for one. A 75-name baseline
> existed until 2026-08-23 and was deleted once measurement showed all 75 were documented here
> anyway, i.e. every exemption was dead while still able to absorb a live one: with it in place,
> *deleting* the row for `MEMRA_SPEC` or `MEMRA_VERIFY_GATE` kept the gate green. Its absence is now
> asserted, so restoring it is a deliberate act needing an owner ruling and an expiry check, not a
> file. Document the flag instead.
>
> Note for whoever writes the next such banner: a family row is written as the backticked name plus
> a trailing `*`, and this paragraph deliberately does not spell one out. An illustrative prefix in
> prose is a LIVE prefix row to the census — the draft of this note used the tcol family as its
> example and would have covered every future `MEMRA_TCOL_` flag while all three existing ones are
> catalogued individually below. Same trap the v0.98 review caught on the dspark family.

Doctrine (CLAUDE.md): **winners are defaults** — naked commands run the tuned path. A flag exists only when it is (a) runtime parameter, (b) machine-specific config, (c) documented rollback seam, (d) diagnostic, or (e) explicitly-blocked experimental door. When an experiment concludes negative or flat, the flag and dispatch arm are deleted — the `research/tune-data/*.jsonl` row is the record, not dead code. The 2026-07-08 flag audit enforced this repo-wide; removed-flag ledger at bottom.

Provenance dates refer to `research/tune-data/rig5090.jsonl` / `sbox-rtx6000.jsonl` rows from
that date. Some older rows also cite lane handover notes; those were archived out of this repo on
2026-08-17 as work coordination, and the dated JSONL rows are the surviving primary source.

Rig names below are receipt provenance, not the current gate roster. The 5090 carries the
development-iteration battery and single-card regression boards; pre-merge/pre-tag validation
and final tuning were measured on 2x RTX PRO 6000 pairs (hardware shape, not a deployment
statement — deployment facts live outside this repo). Existing measurements keep their
original rig labels.

**Coverage, stated honestly** (re-audited 2026-08-19 on v0.92.0, 8b61604cc0): the code has
546 distinct literal `MEMRA_*` names at read sites — `env::var`/`env::var_os`/`env!`/
`option_env!` plus the local env helpers in the Rust tree (including `build.rs`), plus
`getenv` in the CUDA/C sources and the `tools/memra_cpu_experts.cpp` companion. After this
audit every one of them is catalogued in §1-§7 or named in the removed ledger (a few families
use their row's shorthand: `MEMRA_LANE_MAX_INTERACTIVE`/`_JUDGE`/`_HARVEST`,
`MEMRA_PENALTY_REPEAT`/`_FREQ`/`_PRESENT`/`_LAST_N`). The previous "residue lives at its read
sites" policy is retired because the v0.92.0 release found it had drifted — 124 read-site
names had no entry here. The internal ones are still internal:
per-kernel A/B forcers, dump/trace probes, and gate/bench-bin inputs are marked as such in
§3-§5 and are not operator knobs — setting one in a deployment is a measurement act, not a
configuration. Zero live-catalogued flags were found without read sites (the stale direction
of the drift is clean; past removals are all in the ledger below). Do not read a terse entry
as an invitation — the doctrine at the top still holds: winners are defaults, and a naked run
needs none of the internal seams.

Scope note: the merged dsv4 knobs are catalogued individually in §5 below — the
numeric arms (`MEMRA_DSV4_DECODE_PATH`, `MEMRA_DSV4_DENSE_ARM`) and the serve-door
seams (`MEMRA_DSV4_DRAFTER`, `MEMRA_DSV4_DEVICES`, `MEMRA_DSV4_SPEC_DEPTH`,
`MEMRA_DSV4_VT`, `MEMRA_DSV4_VT_TAU`/`_FLOOR`,
lane/dsv4-flash-revival-20260822). The dsv4 door itself is **experimental engine
support, not a serving-grade path** (docs/MODELS.md has the posture and the numbers;
tuning is issue #32). The remaining `MEMRA_DSV4_*` names are that door's bench/gate
instrument seams (bench drivers, NVTX/profile hooks, gate dumps, sweep inputs),
covered here as a prefix — none of them is a serving knob.
Unmerged lane flags stay deliberately absent — they arrive with
their lanes and get catalogued at merge. **The dspark knobs left that list in v0.98.0**: the
q38 dspark lanes merged, so every `MEMRA_DSPARK_` name is now catalogued individually below
(and the census gate treats a backticked `NAME*` as a documented prefix, so keeping a merged
family in this note would blind the gate to its future undocumented flags — the v0.98 review
caught exactly that on `MEMRA_DSPARK_VERIFY_GRAPH`/`_DEFER_READBACK`). **The spec-cache knobs
left that list in v0.95.0**, the same promise kept earlier:
`MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_SAMPLED`, `MEMRA_SPEC_SAMPLED_BOUNDARY`, `MEMRA_SPEC_PEN_SESSION`,
`MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_REPUBLISH`, `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_LOAD_GUARD` and `MEMRA_SPEC_BOUNDARY_TRACE`
arrived with their three lanes and are catalogued in §7 below. The automated census reads 507
literal runtime names at v0.95.0 (501 at v0.94.0 + these six).

**Correction, v0.94.0**: treat 546 as a FLOOR, not a total. That audit was measured with the
same instrument that missed `MEMRA_ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PRETOKENIZER` — the flag deciding whether a
model loads at all — which `tools/check-flags.sh` could not see for two independent reasons:
its crate (`crates/memra-tokenizer/src`) was not in the script's hand-written list of three
runtime dirs, and the read is const-indirected
(`std::env::var(ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PRETOKENIZER_ENV)` carries no `MEMRA_` name on its line). Both
are fixed: the script now discovers every `crates/*/src` and resolves
`const NAME: &str = "MEMRA_…"` bindings that are actually passed to an env call, with
`tools/test_check_flags.sh` gating one regression case per cause in CI. The automated census
went 494 → 501 literal names on that change; the seven it newly sees
(`MEMRA_ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PRETOKENIZER`, `MEMRA_INVALID_PINNED_FRAC_TEST_CHILD`, `MEMRA_FATBIN`,
`MEMRA_FP8_FOLD`, `MEMRA_MOE_MMAP_ADVICE`, `MEMRA_NV_W4`, `MEMRA_ST_TEST_DIR`) were all
already catalogued here, so the gate stayed green — but it stayed green for the wrong reason
before, and the 546 hand count has not been re-run against the widened instrument.

## 1. Runtime parameters

### Generation (run-gen / run-spec / run-eagle)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_PROMPT` | numeric ids `101..612` | prompt TEXT (tokenized with the model's tokenizer) |
| `MEMRA_PROMPT_FILE` | — | prompt text from a file (wins over `MEMRA_PROMPT`) |
| `MEMRA_NGEN` | bin-specific (64–256) | number of tokens to generate |
| `MEMRA_CHAT` | off | `1` wraps the prompt in the model's chat template (run-gen) |
| `MEMRA_PRINT_TEXT` | off | `1` decodes gold tokens between markers (agent-loop harness, run-spec, 2026-07-08) |
| `MEMRA_GEN_ONLY` | off | run-spec: skip prime-inclusive timing, report gen-only |
| `MEMRA_SEED` | 0 | sampler seed (run-gen sampling path) |
| `MEMRA_TEMP` | greedy | temperature; enables the sampling chain |
| `MEMRA_TOP_K` / `MEMRA_TOP_P` / `MEMRA_MIN_P` | off | sampling filters (run-gen) |
| `MEMRA_PENALTY_REPEAT` / `_FREQ` / `_PRESENT` / `_LAST_N` | off | repetition penalties (run-gen) |
| `MEMRA_STOP` | EOS only | comma-separated stop strings (run-gen) |
| `MEMRA_MODELS_DIR` | `~/.cache/memra/models` | where `hf:owner/repo[:file]` specs download to and are looked up in (`hf/<owner>--<repo>/`; `frspec-owngen` also puts datasets under `hf-datasets/`). The one knob for putting weights on a different filesystem — point it at the fast local NVMe rather than symlinking a cache |

### Speculative decode (run-spec / serve)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_SPEC_K` | serve: request policy; `run-spec`: K=1..8 battery | Explicit non-negative values pin draft depth for every eligible request; `0` pins plain decode. With the flag unset, serving chooses per request: `K=0` for measured losing placement/concurrency cells, `K=2` for a long prompt with at least 1024 actually-resumed tokens (`K=5` when the loaded MTP head is FR-Spec rank-TRIMMED — the ~29 us trimmed draft lm_head re-prices the kpolicy-20260808 verdict; ornith15 cached-long 350.8 vs ~273 tok/s, research/orndecode-20260822), and `K=3` otherwise. The cached-long direction is measured, not assumed: K=2 beats K=3 by 1.49% on q9 and 7.11% on q27, N=3. The full table and raw receipts are in `research/kpolicy-20260808/`. `run_hy3_local_5090.sh` additionally accepts `all` to omit its override and run the mandatory K=1..8 battery in one model load |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_HOST_EMBD` | off | `1` keeps the token-embedding table in host memory for the Hy3 spill profile, reducing HBM pressure at the cost of a host-row transfer |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN` | 0.0 | p-min confidence gate — stop the draft chain when head confidence drops below it (0.15–0.3 typical). K-DEPENDENT (2026-08-05, both rigs): at K=5 on 188 SM it's worth +6.1% e2e (0.3 optimum, 0.2/0.4 both worse); at the 5090's K=3 serve optimum it's FLAT-to-negative despite the same +4–15pp acceptance jump — a K=3 chain is too short for gating to pay. Exactness clean both rigs (byte-identical greedy, run-spec 9 PASS lever-on). Receipts: `research/spec-levers-5090-20260805/`, `research/q27-deepdive-p2-20260805/` |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN0` | off | `1` lets p-min gate slot 0 too (zero-draft rounds). Pays below ~75% base acceptance, hurts above ~90% (2026-07-08, 35B +13–23 tok/s) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_HPOST` | off | `1` feeds the MTP head POST-output_norm hidden — acceptance lever on the 27B (>100 tok/s crossing, 2026-07-06). Per-model choice |
| `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` | off | `<ranks.txt>`, `<frspec.gguf>`, or `hf:owner/repo:file-substring`: self-trimmed draft lm_head — gathers top-frequency rows from the model's own `output.weight` via the d2t ranking. An `hf:` spec resolves into `MEMRA_MODELS_DIR` like a model path, so the q38 DFlash2 default can fetch its rank artifact declaratively. A **ranks `.txt`** does the trim at load time, which is the safetensors path and needs no separate draft file; a `.gguf` carries a pre-trimmed head. Generic rankings transfer across same-vocab heads, specialised ones do not (2026-07-07). Published ranks: Qwen3.8-27B agentic/prose/mixed in [Avifenesh/Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-MTP-GGUF](https://huggingface.co/Avifenesh/Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-MTP-GGUF); Gemma family under `research/gemma4-bringup/*-ranks-*.txt` Since lane/dflash2-head-trim (2026-08-25) the DSPARK/DFlash2 selector round consumes the same self-trim BY DEFAULT when it is loaded: the round's draft lm_head matmul + top-k run over the gathered rows and candidates remap through d2t before the selector walk — verify stays full-vocab (exactness unchanged; boot receipt `[dspark] <model>: DFlash2 draft head TRIMMED to N rows`). Measured PRO 6000 x3: gate trim-q4 160.4 vs full-q4 157.9 spec tok/s; served steady chat 142.9 vs 136.3 / agentic 157.2 vs 154.5 (accept 0.64/0.73 — the wall wins the acceptance trade). Markov/chain drafter families keep the full head by construction. |
| `MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT` | off | **QWEN/step35 MTP ATTACH — not the gemma one, see the seam trap below.** `<draft.gguf>`: replace the MTP head with a standalone draft GGUF (exactness unaffected — verify arbitrates). The standard artifact is the regime draft (docs/DRAFT-REGIME.md): own-gen ranks + NVFP4 head + Q4_K_M block, one file, no other flags. Per-model equivalent on the server: `MEMRA_MODELS="name=/trunk.gguf+/draft.gguf"`. This is ALSO how step35 attaches its published external MTP head (Step-3.7-Flash ships the NextN blocks in a separate GGUF, so the trunk reads `nextn=0`); a step35 trunk served WITHOUT one WARNS at load, and a `+draft` path that is missing or unloadable REFUSES to start with the cause quoted rather than degrading to plain decode. (#87's spec-over-PP-2 parse-time refusal was removed when the quarantine lifted 2026-08-08 — see `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC`.) **PROOF OF ATTACH is a log line, not the absence of an error:** `[mtp-draft] loading external MTP draft: <path>` (this flag) or `[worker] <name>: regime draft attached (<path>)` (the `+draft` spelling). Receipts `research/step-draft-20260807/` |
| `MEMRA_DRAFT` | off | **GEMMA ASSISTANT-DRAFTER ATTACH — and three silent engine-default flips.** `<assistant.gguf>`, consumed with `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC=K`: a separate small gemma model drafts over the trunk's KV (`gspec`). It does **NOT** attach an MTP head; on a qwen/step35 model it attaches nothing at all. What it *does* do everywhere, by mere presence, is re-key three defaults onto "spec serving": `memra_kv::wkv_on()` → **OFF** (gemma windowed KV leaves e4m3 for q8_0/q5_1 — `memra-kv/src/lib.rs`), `fa_f16pv_on()` → **OFF** (f16 P/V prefill class — `memra-engine/src/lib.rs`), and `MMQ_SK_FORCE` → tiling for big dense `n_embd ≥ 3500` at model load (`hybrid.rs`). Each is separately measured and separately correct (see the `MEMRA_GEMMA_WKV` / `MEMRA_FA_F16PV` / `MEMRA_MMQ_SK` rows), and each is *silent*. Explicit `MEMRA_GEMMA_WKV` / `MEMRA_FA_F16PV` / `MEMRA_MMQ_SK` always win over the flip. |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_DRAFT_RANKS` | off | `<ranks.txt>`: gemma FR-Spec drafter head trim — corpus-ranked vocab ids, head gathered to those rows (150→18 MB at 32k). ADOPTED for 26B serving: +2.8% short/+5.8% depth spec at IDENTICAL acceptance (2026-07-12; ranks file: `research/gemma4-bringup/gemma4-frspec-ranks-32768.txt`). Historical negative verdicts were measured through two since-fixed verify bugs |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_TRIM_ADAPT` | 512 | spare head slots for the serve-time adaptive trim (needs `..._DRAFT_RANKS`; `0` = static trim). Coverage escapes — tokens the trim can't propose — are the entire trim cost (oracle 2026-07-19); the loop learns them from prompt ids + verify argmaxes and persists to `<ranks>.learned`, so a distribution's escapes pay their first-miss round once per lifetime, not per request. Flipped the 31B chat cell from −17% to +2.5% and made trim ≥ untrimmed on EVERY measured cell (2026-07-19) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_TEMP` | greedy (0.0) | speculative sampling temperature — the spec path's own knob, NOT `MEMRA_TEMP`. Above 0 switches the round from greedy verify to Leviathan/Chen modified rejection sampling (`norm(max(0,p-q))` on reject, bonus drawn from `p` on full accept), and takes `MEMRA_SEED` plus the `MEMRA_PENALTY_*` set through the same `SpecSampling` struct. Its gate is seeded REPRODUCIBILITY, not token-identity to plain sampling: spec and plain consume randomness differently, so Leviathan/Chen guarantees equality of DISTRIBUTION only — `run-spec` asserts the same (seed, prompt, K) reproduces an identical stream on a second run |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_CAPMAX` | 7 | gemma adaptive-K cap ceiling. >7 opens the b16 verify tier (t=9..16) — correct since 2026-07-12 (three host bugs fixed) but measured perf-negative (depth K8-10 283-293 vs K6 301-306); the door is a measurement, not a crash |

#### SEAM TRAP: `MEMRA_DRAFT` is the GEMMA drafter. `MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT` is the QWEN one.

Two flags, one word apart, attaching different mechanisms to different families. Getting it
wrong does not error — **it passes.**

| you want | flag | equivalent | log line that PROVES it |
|---|---|---|---|
| qwen / step35 MTP head from a separate GGUF (e.g. the FR-Spec drafter) | `MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT=<draft.gguf>` | `MEMRA_MODELS="name=<trunk>+<draft>"` | `[mtp-draft] loading external MTP draft: …` / `[worker] <name>: regime draft attached (…)` |
| gemma-4 assistant drafter over trunk KV | `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC=K` **+** `MEMRA_DRAFT=<assistant.gguf>` | — | the gemma draft-attach line at spawn; boot PANICS if `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC` is set without `MEMRA_DRAFT` |

**How this went wrong, so the shape is recognisable (96GB window, 2026-08-19).** A qwen
27B cell was launched with the gemma flag. The server logged `models config = [(…, None)]`
and **no `[mtp-draft]` line** — the drafter was never loaded. Spec still engaged, off the
trunk's own embedded MTP head, so **every assertion in the cell went green** while the thing
the cell existed to test ("the frspec drafter loads and drafts") was never exercised. Two
compounding reasons it was invisible: an unused `MEMRA_DRAFT` on a non-gemma model is not an
error, and a qwen trunk that already carries `nextn>0` has a working drafter *without* the
file — so the negative control was silently unavailable.

Rules that follow, and are now enforced rather than remembered:

- **A gate tool handed a drafter path MUST assert the attach line in the server log.** A run
  with no `[mtp-draft]` / `regime draft attached` line is a FAILED run, not a passing one —
  see `tools/assert-drafter-attached.sh`, wired into `spec-on-cache-hit-gate.sh`,
  `spec-cache-gate.sh`, `spec-cache-mixed-gate.sh`, `sampled-restore-ab.sh` and
  `btemp-power.sh`. The qwen seam in every one of them is `MEMRA_GATE_MTP_DRAFT=<f>`
  (forwarded as `MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT`); `spec-cache-mixed-gate.sh`'s positional 4th argument
  is the GEMMA drafter and the tool now refuses if both are given.
- **Never infer the attach from spec engaging.** `usage.spec.accepted > 0` on a qwen trunk
  proves the *embedded* head ran; it says nothing about an external drafter.
- **`MEMRA_DRAFT` on a non-gemma run is a config error, not a no-op** — it still flips
  `wkv_on()`, `fa_f16pv_on()` and the MMQ-SK form, so such a run is not even measuring the
  production kernel/format defaults.

### Benchmark harness (run-gen / session-bench)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_PP_ONLY` | off | prefill-only timing mode (times `prime_cache` — the pass serving primes with, and the ONLY prefill class that carries the m≥16 GEMM/MMQ hooks; see `MEMRA_PP_NLL`) |
| `MEMRA_PP_REPS` | 1 | prefill repetitions (N-median protocol) |
| `MEMRA_PP_WARMUP` | 1 | prefill warmup runs |
| `MEMRA_PP_LOGITS` | off | `<file>`: under `MEMRA_PP_ONLY`, dump `prime_cache`'s last-row logits as raw LE f32 — the cross-arm drift vector (max_abs / rms_rel / top-k order) for a **prefill-GEMM** arm |
| `MEMRA_PP_NLL` | off | `1`: under `MEMRA_PP_ONLY`, teacher-forced prefill exactness on the prime path — argmax disagreement + mean NLL where **the prompt itself is the tape** (position `i`'s logits score prompt token `i+1`). No reference-tape asymmetry by construction: both arms are scored on the same externally-given sequence, so neither can win by reproducing its own output (contrast the decode battery, whose tape is one arm's greedy output and therefore needs a reverse-tape control). WHY IT EXISTS: `run-gen`'s verify-prefill gate calls `decode_step_t` → `matmul_decode_exact`, which has NO GEMM/MMQ arm by design (decode-parity law), so `MEMRA_PREFILL_LOGITS` is **blind** to a prefill-GEMM arm — it reports identical vectors and `hook entries=0` no matter what flag is set. Added 2026-08-05 after that exact trap invalidated a first pass at the block-128 MMQ cells |
| `MEMRA_PREFILL_LOGITS` | off | `<file>`: dump the VERIFY-class (`decode_step_t`) batched-prefill logit row as raw LE f32. Cross-arm instrument for a kernel that changes the verify class — **not** for a prefill-GEMM arm (use `MEMRA_PP_NLL` / `MEMRA_PP_LOGITS`) |
| `MEMRA_NMEASURE` | 32 | decode tokens measured after counter reset |
| `MEMRA_FORCE_TOKENS_FILE` | off | run-gen diagnostic: teacher-force numeric token ids from a file so placement A/Bs traverse an identical decode route. Also the decode-side branch-(b) exactness instrument (disagreement count + tape NLL); pair it with a REVERSE-TAPE control run, since a tape that is one arm's own greedy output structurally favours that arm |
| `MEMRA_FORCE_LOGITS_AT` / `MEMRA_FORCE_LOGITS_FILE` | off | run-gen diagnostic used with `MEMRA_FORCE_TOKENS_FILE`: dump the raw LE f32 logit row that predicts the zero-based forced-tape position named by `_AT`. Both variables and the forcing tape are required, so an incomplete probe cannot silently measure a different trajectory |
| `MEMRA_TURN_TOKENS` | 512 | session-bench: suffix length per turn |
| `MEMRA_HIST_FILE` | required | session-bench: conversation history text file |

### Server (memra-server)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_MODELS` | — | server model list `alias=path[+draft.gguf],...` — `+draft` attaches that model's regime draft per model (docs/DRAFT-REGIME.md); paths accept `hf:` specs. A path may also be an HF safetensors checkpoint DIR (`config.json` + `model.safetensors[.index.json]`, chat template from `tokenizer_config.json`/`chat_template.jinja`) or a memra repack dir (`manifest.json`) — validated at startup, template-less dir ckpts 400 on chat (serve-st lane, `research/serve-st-20260803/`) |
| `MEMRA_REWRITE_BUNDLE` | none | Path to a sealed `memra model inspect`/`verify` bundle. At model load the worker validates the artifact lock, compiled-plan hash, rewrite manifest, indexed receipt hashes, and each receipt's artifact/plan binding before admitting an optimized rewrite. An absent bundle keeps the legacy native policy; an invalid configured bundle fails model load. Optional unqualified batch/graph/prime surfaces may use the bundle's qualified native-eager rewrite, while required speculative and pipeline selections fail closed. |
| `MEMRA_MODEL_METADATA` | none | TOML metadata for strict marketplace feeds: `GET /models?schema=openrouter` (OpenRouter Provider Monitor 2.4) and `GET /models?schema=openmodels` (OpenModels provider shape). `[models."<MEMRA_MODELS alias>"]` declares description/quantization/per-request limits/operational fields, with nested `.pricing` per-unit USD strings and `.capacity` limits. Declared `max_output_length` bounds explicit `max_tokens`; optional `default_output_length` separately bounds an omitted value so a large provider ceiling does not force a large KV reservation. Declared `max_prompt_length` is checked against the worker-rendered/tokenized prompt before cache lookup or GPU admission, and every request remains capped by trained model context. Optional `default_reasoning_effort` (`none|minimal|low|medium|high`, boot-validated) resolves requests that expressed no reasoning choice as if the client had sent that value; explicit client reasoning fields always win. Optional per-model VENDOR SAMPLING defaults — `default_temperature` (`(0,2]`), `default_top_p` (`(0,1]`), `default_top_k` (`>=0`), `default_min_p` (`[0,1)`), `default_presence_penalty` / `default_frequency_penalty` (`[-2,2]`), `default_repetition_penalty` (`>0`), all boot-validated — substitute the model vendor's own published recommendation into sampling fields the client OMITTED, identically on all four surfaces (`/v1/completions`, `/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/messages`, `/v1/responses`); an explicit client value always wins, and an explicit `temperature: 0` still produces true greedy. `default_temperature = 0` is REFUSED at boot: a zero default would reinstate greedy-by-default deployment-wide for every omitting client. Undeclared parameters keep the API-standard default rather than an invented one. An optional nested `.non_thinking_sampling` table (same seven keys unprefixed, same ranges/boot validation, empty table refused) declares the vendor's SECOND arm: a request whose RESOLVED thinking mode is off (any off spelling, an operator `default_reasoning_effort = "none"`, or a `response_format`-forced switch) takes that arm for its unset sampling fields; models without the table are byte-identical to before it existed, and the arms never blend field-by-field. Both these key sets are `deny_unknown_fields`, so an OLDER binary FAILS BOOT on a config declaring them — deploy the binary before the config. `[planned_models]` entries are validated but never emitted or served. OpenRouter omits undeclared operator fields; OpenModels rejects missing required fields with HTTP 400. Values are never invented or zero-filled; bad files or active aliases fail before GPU load. See docs/SERVING.md "Gateway listing surface" |
| `MEMRA_REQUEST_LEDGER` | none | Optional path for the durable append-only request/cost JSONL ledger. Enabling it requires complete prompt, cached-prompt, and completion prices in `MEMRA_MODEL_METADATA`; invalid or missing tariff metadata fails startup. Terminal rows are synced before a non-streaming 200 or streaming terminal event and record tenant, request/model/lane, outcome, exact ordinary/cached/completion usage, and decimal USD costs without request bodies, credentials, or cache salts. Abandoned (client-disconnect) rows carry the partial delivered-token usage and cost. A write failure latches the ledger unhealthy and prevents an unreceipted success. |
| `MEMRA_CAPTURE_DIR` | none (capture off) | Default-off per-tenant request capture (`memra-server/src/capture.rs`). Set to a directory and tenants explicitly marked through the admin surface get their SETTLED request/response pairs appended as JSONL under it (dir is chmod 0750, `capture-<n>.jsonl` rotation). The mark carries a reason chosen by provisioning (`trial` or `consent`) — the server never infers one, an unmarked tenant is never captured, and clearing the mark stops capture immediately (re-read at settle time). A `trial` tenant is captured only while its settled prepaid balance is non-negative (under-capture by construction). Requires `MEMRA_REQUEST_LEDGER` — boot refuses without it. Operator knob with data-handling weight: this writes customer payloads to disk; treat the directory like the ledger (private, 0750, never synced off-box) |
| `MEMRA_TENANT_BUDGETS` | none | Optional private regular TOML (`[[budgets]]`) or JSONL append-only tenant-credit source (0600/0640 class). Each row is `{tenant, currency="USD", balance_micro}` and contributes that many integer micro-USD; duplicate tenant rows add. A source or admin-credit row enrolls the tenant in prepaid enforcement (including a zero source row), while tenants absent from both remain unlimited. Before worker admission the engine reserves `ceil(ordinary prompt price × exact rendered prompt tokens + completion price × maximum possible completion tokens + request price)` in integer micro-USD; insufficient funds return 402, and terminal Decimal cost settles/refunds the hold. The source is revalidated within 2 s; reloads may only append/increase totals, and three consecutive failed polls fail admissions closed at 503. Requires `MEMRA_REQUEST_LEDGER`; see docs/SERVING.md "Self-serve seams". |
| `MEMRA_ADMIN_ADDR` | off | Enables the separate provisioning listener, for example `127.0.0.1:8005`. It must resolve only to loopback and requires `MEMRA_ADMIN_TOKEN_FILE`, file-backed `MEMRA_API_KEYS`, `MEMRA_REQUEST_LEDGER`, and `MEMRA_TENANT_BUDGETS`; invalid combinations fail before model/GPU initialization. This listener is never mounted on `MEMRA_ADDR`. |
| `MEMRA_ADMIN_TOKEN_FILE` | none | Path to a private regular file (0600/0640 class) containing the admin listener's bearer token. It is read at startup, never accepted as a CLI argument, and required whenever `MEMRA_ADMIN_ADDR` is enabled. Missing authorization returns 401; malformed or incorrect authorization returns 403. |
| `MEMRA_ADDR` | `127.0.0.1:8080` | listen address. A non-loopback address is a startup FATAL unless `MEMRA_API_KEY` or `MEMRA_API_KEYS` is configured; validation happens before model/GPU initialization |
| `MEMRA_ALLOW_OPEN_BIND` | off | `1` explicitly permits a no-key non-loopback development bind. Completion routes are open in that shape, but `/metrics` and `/yield/metrics` still require `MEMRA_METRICS_TOKEN`; never use this as a production auth substitute |
| `MEMRA_API_KEY` | none | single static bearer key (the daily driver); setting it also defaults `MEMRA_COMPAT=openai`. Authenticates as tenant `default` and, without `MEMRA_API_KEYS` or `MEMRA_METRICS_TOKEN`, authorizes completion and both legacy metrics views. Composes with `MEMRA_API_KEYS` — both may be live at once, with the keyring deployment's tenant-only metrics policy applying to every completion credential |
| `MEMRA_API_KEYS` | none | multi-key tenant keyring (lane/api-keys, 2026-08-05): a `keys.toml` path (entries store SHA-256 hex + tenant + lane class + optional `rate_limit`; hot-reloads on mtime, ≤2s poll; atomic revoke rewrites prevent readers from observing a truncated ring) or an inline `tenant:sha256hex[:lane]` list. Key → tenant identity: PC-ISO cache namespace becomes `t:<tenant>␟<cache_salt>` (a tenant's keys share cache; tenants never cross), batch-class keys default to harvest and are 403'd off `x-lane: interactive`, per-key `rate_limit` = min(override, lane cap) in the X-RateLimit headers, and `[meter]` admit lines carry the tenant id. Without `MEMRA_METRICS_TOKEN`, a key authorizes `/metrics` but receives only its own tenant rows; process-wide counters and `/yield/metrics` are withheld. Configure the dedicated metrics token for the full operator surface; completion keys then receive 403 on both routes. Manage with `memra-server --gen-key <tenant> [--lane batch] [--rate-limit N]` / `--revoke-key <prefix>` (docs/SERVING.md "API keys") |
| `MEMRA_METRICS_TOKEN` | none | exclusive `Authorization: Bearer <token>` credential for the process-wide `/metrics` and `/yield/metrics` operator surfaces; when configured, completion API keys receive 403 on both routes and the scrape token sees all tenant rows. A multi-key `MEMRA_API_KEYS` deployment defaults to tenant-only `/metrics` and withholds `/yield/metrics` until this token is configured. It does not authorize completion routes. Metrics require a bearer whenever any API-key source or this token is configured, or whenever `MEMRA_ADDR` is non-loopback. Only a no-key loopback development server exposes metrics without auth |
| `MEMRA_COMPAT` | native (`openai` when API_KEY set) | response shape: `openai` = OpenAI completions SSE |
| `MEMRA_CTX` | 8192 | fallback session-cache context when a request omits both `max_ctx` and a finite `max_tokens`. An explicit `max_ctx` is authoritative; without it, a finite request uses `prompt_tokens + max_tokens + 8`. KV @8192 ≈ 119MB/session on the 9B. Machine envelope: on the 24GB 5090 under the q8rp-mirror config, c=32 ctx-8192 sessions OOM (captured `CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY`; ~27 admit) — set the fallback to the unbounded-request workload, 2048 clears the same cell at 502 tok/s (`research/batched-tick-inc3-20260801/`) |
| `MEMRA_DRAIN_S` | 30 | graceful-drain deadline (serve-tail lane, 2026-08-04): on SIGTERM the server 503s new completion requests (`code: draining`, `Retry-After` = this value clamped to 60 s, with the matching `retry-after-ms`), in-flight requests (streams included) finish up to this many seconds, then the process exits 0. During the drain `/health` stays **200** with `status:"draining"` (a drain is a healthy, deliberate shutdown — 503 there would invite a supervisor to SIGKILL a process mid-stream) while `/readyz` answers **503**, which is what takes the instance out of rotation. Keep this below `TimeoutStopSec` in the systemd unit; the server also sends `EXTEND_TIMEOUT_USEC` under `Type=notify` |
| `MEMRA_RL_RESET_S` | 2 | static `X-RateLimit-Reset` fallback (seconds) when all slots are taken and the live meter has no signal yet; with signal, Reset = mean tokens/request x p50 step latency (honestly coarse) |
| `MEMRA_MAX_QUEUE_DEPTH` | 4 × `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS` | absolute interactive-queue bound — the backpressure BACKSTOP (lane/deadline-billing, 2026-08-23). The interactive lane admits up to the session cap and queues beyond it; past this many queued requests a new one is SHED with `429` + `Retry-After` (`code: shed_queue`, ledger outcome `shed_queue`, **never billed**) instead of joining a queue it cannot be served from. Before this bound a saturated box accepted everything and merely answered later, which is what the router's breaker cannot act on. The deadline test (estimated wait vs the request's remaining `timeout_ms`) sheds first and is the primary gate; this is the load-shape-independent ceiling. An already-admitted request is never shed. Raise it for bursty clients that would rather wait than retry; lower it to fail fast |
| `MEMRA_KV_REUSE` | on | `0` disables the KV prefix-reuse pool (session-gate validated; 42.6x turn-start at 40k) |
| `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL` | 2 | per-`(model, cache namespace)` LRU cap, applied independently to the continuation, speculative (MTP), and DFlash2/dspark whole-session pools; see [PC-ISO](SERVING.md#cache-salt-isolation). The dspark pool clamps EOS and `max_tokens` rounds to the public committed prefix before park; a max-token terminal resumes only when the next request contributes a non-empty suffix. A request whose ctx cap outgrew the parked allocation still serves cold. Before the process ceiling, one populated tier could retain roughly `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL × distinct live cache_salt namespaces × parked-entry bytes`: ~119 MB/entry at ctx 8192 on the 9B, but multi-GB spec entries at long context. At the default, sequential workloads of >2 sessions in one namespace hit the park-evicts-next-entry cascade (0/N resumes; pinned 2026-07-31); raise only with a matching global VRAM budget. `0` = park nothing (pooling off). |
| `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL_GLOBAL_CAP` | 16 | process-wide maximum parked entries across every model, cache namespace, and all three whole-session pools: continuation, MTP-spec, and DFlash2/dspark. Before each park that would exceed it, the worker drops the globally oldest entry across all three pools; failure to produce a slot refuses the insertion, and the per-namespace cap above still applies. Size as `floor(parked VRAM budget / worst-case parked-entry bytes)` and lower it for long-context/multi-GB entries. `0` = park nothing process-wide. Default 16 preserves the measured [27bab two-namespace Q27 shape](../research/27bab-20260810/RESULTS.md#vram-ledger): 16 spec entries, zero OOM parks, 27.34 GB driver-free; the salt-per-turn control reached 76 entries and a captured CUDA OOM before this server-side ceiling existed. |
| `MEMRA_AFFINITY` | on | `0` disables session-affinity resume (docs/SERVING.md "Session affinity"): a conversation whose history the client REWROTE (think-strip) no longer resumes its parked session and re-primes in full — the pre-2026-08-05 behavior. Rollback seam / exactness A/B arm, not a tuning knob; prefer it over `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL=0` for that comparison, since killing the pool also kills the token/text-prefix resumes. Receipts `research/session-affinity-20260805/` |
> **Qwen35-MoE exactness repair (2026-08-14):** verification now uses the live serving numeric
> class, and every accepted round rebuilds committed cache state through sequential serving steps.
> The production IQ4_XS long-prompt gate is bit-identical at verifier widths T=1..4 for 128 rounds,
> K=1..8 sessions match plain for all 128 generated tokens, and the OpenAI serving envelope matches
> with spec forced ON/OFF. This correctness reference path is intentionally conservative; benchmark
> it before making any workload-specific performance claim.
| `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC` | on (GGUF and dir ckpts) | `0` disables the spec-decode serve path (greedy + MTP-head requests). **PRODUCTION POSTURE IS `0` ON SHARED-PREFIX SERVING SHAPES, measured 2026-08-13** — spec is numerically EXACT (content+reasoning SHA-256 identical to spec-off) but it FORFEITS the cross-request prefix cache, and on a cache-carried shape the cache is worth far more than the decode gain. Same box, same fresh cache, sold shape (4,860-token prompt + 60 out, 90% shared prefix): spec-off c=16 = 8.50 req/s at p50 1.87 s with 99.5% cache hit, spec-on c=16 = 2.14 req/s at p50 7.40 s with 18.4% hit — a **4x loss** (`research/canonflip-20260813/`, commit 753978e96). The CLI picture is the opposite and is what misleads: `run-spec` shows 1.51-1.70x vs plain at K=4..8 with 51.8-80.0% acceptance, because it has no cross-request cache to give up. So keep spec for cache-POOR shapes (cold/unique prompts) and keep it OFF where a shared prefix carries the prefill; do not re-enable it from the CLI numbers alone. Q35-MoE's exact serving path currently replays accepted bursts sequentially; constrained Q35 requests therefore fail closed to the exact plain constrained path because replay cannot commit a grammar-masked bonus. A cache-safe spec design is open work (see the prefix-cache rows and the spec_pool metrics). The 2026-08-03 dir-checkpoint quarantine is LIFTED (#68 closed 2026-08-04): the divergence was the per-session persistent draft graph replaying with dangling pool addresses (capture transients not retained + `fa_part_pool` freeing grown-past buffers the capture baked) — never ST-specific (reproduced on GGUF session bursts at n>=600). Fixed via capture-retain keepers on `DraftGraphCtx` + retire-on-grow for the fa partials pool; gate: `tools/serve-st-gate.sh` item 4 pins the DEFAULT (spec-on) server against the tokenwise **serve** oracle — `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC=0 MEMRA_SERVE_BATCH=0` on the same worker, 400-token window, prefix-tolerant for burst overshoot. The comparator is deliberately the serve oracle and not the `run-gen` CLI: both the batched-plain path and the CLI carry their own accepted near-tie FP classes at long windows (item 3 is the CLI comparison, at a 64-token window). Do not restate item 4 as "token-identical to the CLI oracle". Receipts `research/fp8ship-20260804/RESULTS.md`. **The PP-2 quarantine is LIFTED (#87 closed 2026-08-08)**: the sticky `CUDA_ERROR_ILLEGAL_ADDRESS` was the ppN reverse-publication hole, fixed by `PpNRt::fence_stages_behind` at all three ppN bodies + stage-cache admission, with sentinel traps as containment. Crash gate: c=2 8/8, c=4 100/100, c=8 104/104; run-spec K=1..8 PASS; ppspec bit-identity 0 failing arms (`research/pp2spec-crash-20260807/`). The later v0.72 head-affinity fix made both PP-2 placement orders the same 111-112 tok/s spec class; correctness is live, but the placement-aware spec gate now defaults PP-2 admission OFF because plain batched serving wins every measured c=1/2/4 cell. `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE=0` still forces spec for rollback and the #87 crash gate. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE` | on, placement-aware | `0` is the rollback seam: always-spec at every concurrency and placement. With the gate on, single-card keeps the measured scheduler from task #89: admit spec while `active+1 <= LOW=2`, demote live greedy spec sessions at `active >= HIGH=4`, and hand `(cache, next_pred)` to batched decode after flushing carried pending. Current-train q9 re-sweep: spec ON/OFF = 374.8/224.5 at c=1, 374.5/347.5 at c=2, 377.3/617.1 at c=4 (N=3; c=2 ranges do not overlap), so the crossover still lies between 2 and 4. Sharded cross-device PP-2 defaults to LOW=0/HIGH=1 (never admit spec): inherited q9 = 112 vs 223/340/593 at c=1/2/4; current step35 = 35.9/36.2/36.7 vs 85.7/101.6/121.7 (N=3). Greedy demotion exactness remains the task #89 proof; sampled and constrained sessions are excluded from demotion. Receipts `research/spec-gate-20260806/`, `research/pp2spec-crash-20260807/`, `research/specplace-20260808/`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE_LOW` / `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE_HIGH` | single/non-PP2: 2 / 4; sharded PP-2: 0 / 1 | Admit-spec ceiling and demote floor. Explicit values override the placement defaults. The single-card 2/4 pair is the last measured WIN rung and first measured LOSS rung; `act == 3` is the hysteresis band, and one-way demotion bounds switches to O(load crossings). PP-2's 0/1 pair expresses the measured all-OFF verdict; LOW=0 admits no spec session and avoids charging the spec transient admission reserve. Set LOW=1/HIGH=2 only to test a c=1-only PP-2 policy; it is not the default because c=1 lost on both q9 and step35. `HIGH <= LOW` is clamped to `LOW+1` with a loud warning. |
| `MEMRA_OPTI_CONTROLLER_Q` | absent (**NO-GO**) | Fresh-process-only diagnostic door for the PP-2 depth-1 optimistic controller; setting a finite threshold in inclusive `[0,1]` arms it globally for that worker, while invalid values fail startup. Keep absent in production: every corrected q arm lost to serial, the merged seam, and plain because even rejected opportunities pay an online shadow-draft step. Revival requires all four gates in [opti2 RESULTS](../research/opti2-20260811/RESULTS.md#what-would-change-the-verdict): a trained/calibrated prefix-survival selector frozen from non-public retained traces; a low-confidence path byte- and performance-equivalent to plain with no shadow step; a retained segment whose conservative lower bound clears the re-solved economic threshold; then interleaved N=5 admitted-segment win plus zero low-q regression and the full 2x PRO 6000 pre-release battery. |
| `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS` | 64 | max concurrently-active INTERACTIVE sessions under batched scheduling (admits beyond it queue FIFO, never rejected). Legacy round-robin mode (`MEMRA_SERVE_BATCH=0`) uses the fixed MAX_ACTIVE=4 bound instead. Judge/harvest lanes cap separately (`MEMRA_LANE_MAX_*`) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_OOM_RETRIES` | 3 | step-time CUDA-OOM PARK budget per request (lane/admit-oom, 2026-08-06). A spec-burst step that OOMs despite admission rebuilds its request and re-queues at the FRONT instead of erroring the stream; the retire sweep that follows is what frees the VRAM. Parks only a session that has emitted NOTHING (restarting a streamed session would replay its prefix to the client) and only on a QUOTED CUDA OOM; anything else reports honestly and logs why it was not parked. Parking costs a re-prime — pure latency, never a correctness change. `0` = rollback seam (pre-fix kill-on-OOM). Receipted load-bearing on its own: at a deliberately-broken reserve it salvaged 11/64 where the pre-fix path lost 64/64 (`research/admit-oom-20260806/`) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_RESERVE_MB` | unset (1536) | **teeth/diagnostics door, not a tuning knob** — overrides the spec-session admission transient reserve. The admission gate charges `SPEC_SHRINK_RESERVE` only when the current placement policy can actually admit spec; PP-2's default LOW=0 is plain-only and keeps the legacy `cost` headroom term. That 1.5 GiB floor closed the single-card c=64 red (0/64 well-formed → 64/64). `tools/serve-stress-gate.sh --teeth` can force it tiny (16MB) and prove the gate still catches a broken cost model — a gate only ever observed passing proves nothing. Warns loudly when set. |
| `MEMRA_CI_STRESS` | on | `0` skips the c=64 serve-stress gate in `tools/local-ci.sh` (`tools/serve-stress-gate.sh`: 64 staggered streaming clients, asserts all complete + well-formed + worker alive + clean log). In-battery since the admission fix; it was deliberately left unwired while red |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_BURST` | 32 | tokens per spec burst — round-robin/streaming latency vs per-burst fixed cost (pending-flush + init-feed amortization). The 2026-07-06 "throughput-neutral" null was pre-persistent-draft-graph; re-measured 2026-08-05 on BOTH tiers: `128` is the throughput optimum — 5090/82 SM +7% c=1 (512-tok), +6.9% c=8, +9.2% on the 9B, curve turns DOWN at 256; PRO 6000/188 SM +7.5% (B64 flat there, not here). Byte-identical greedy both rigs. The old flip-blocker — ONE SSE event per burst (at 128: 2 chunks/1.15s first text vs 8 chunks/0.41s at 32) — is FIXED (sse-cadence, 2026-08-05): the worker flushes text per spec-round commit, so first text is ~0.12s and cadence ~27ms at ANY burst size for a solo stream. The SECOND flip-blocker — burst size set round-robin admission latency (contended first-text 0.57s at B32 vs 1.67s at B128) — is also FIXED (admission yield, 2026-08-06): a pending admit ends the in-flight burst at the round boundary and cold sessions burst first, so contended first-text is 0.123s(B32)/0.152s(B128), the solo class at any burst. Kept at 32 by the strict flip criterion: B128 contended is one round-cadence quantum (29ms) behind B32 while buying +8.4%(c=1)/+8.5%(c=8) — a live owner call, no longer a cliff. Set `128` on throughput-tier serve configs (c≥2 batch, judge/harvest, non-streaming API); leave default for the daily pill. **Not** the lever for the mixed-tick penalty: bounding the burst to recover the batched rows' latency was measured and REFUTED (lane/spec-gate, 2026-08-07). In an un-demoted mixed tick at c=6, `MEMRA_SPEC_BURST=4` leaves stream p95 at 11.075 s against the default-32 arm's 11.243 s — inside per-rep spread — and costs 1.4% aggregate throughput. The tick shape says why: mixed ticks go 17 → 109, i.e. the same total serial work sliced thinner, because phase (a) still runs every spec session's rounds before phase (c) is reached. Demotion (`MEMRA_SPEC_GATE`) is the fix that works there (p95 → 7.249 s). Receipts: `research/spec-levers-5090-20260805/`, `research/sse-cadence-20260805/`, `research/admission-20260806/`, `research/spec-gate-20260806/` |
| `MEMRA_SSE_PER_BURST` | off | `1` restores pre-2026-08-05 per-burst SSE emission (one `Event::Token` per spec burst) — rollback seam for the round-cadence streaming fix. Content is byte-identical either way (same detokenize-tail + utf8_delta cursor; gate receipts `research/sse-cadence-20260805/logs/ident-*`); only chunk boundaries change. Throughput parity measured c=1 and c=8 (fix-on 100.1/98.6 vs fix-off 100.0/98.4 agg tok/s, B128 q27) |
| `MEMRA_SSE_COALESCE` | off / unwired | No runtime reader exists on current main: SSE keeps one encoded event per body write. The 8-frame / 16 KiB / 32 ms coalescing experiment remained in scratch work and is not a serving default. Do not wire or default it on without a real SSE-client gate reporting ITL p50/p95/p99 (including `[DONE]`) in addition to TTFB and aggregate throughput. |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_YIELD` | on | `0` restores pre-2026-08-06 admission behavior — rollback seam for the admission-latency fix (both pieces: full-burst holds AND index-order spec stepping). Default-on: a request arriving mid-burst ends the in-flight spec burst at the next round boundary (the `PENDING_ADMITS` gauge, polled by the sse-cadence round hook) and not-yet-emitted sessions burst before mid-generation peers, so contended first-text no longer scales with `MEMRA_SPEC_BURST` (B128: 1.60s → 0.152s; B32: 0.54s → 0.123s — the solo class). Content byte-identical on/off, solo AND contended, background response included. Costs at c=8 saturation: −3.4% agg tok/s for 3.8x better p50 (newcomer-first vs lockstep-fair; p95 tail pays). c=1 parity. Set `0` on pure-batch tiers that want lockstep fairness back. Receipts `research/admission-20260806/` |
| `MEMRA_SLO_P99_MS` | 50 | x-lane QoS gate (lane/qos-p95, 2026-08-02 — the dl-metering QoS extraction): the interactive decode-step p99 SLO. Requests tag a class via the `x-lane` header (`interactive`\|`judge`\|`harvest`; absent = interactive — naked traffic is unaffected). Interactive always admits; judge/harvest SHED at admission (HTTP 429 + Retry-After) when measured interactive step p99 crosses `MEMRA_SHED_JUDGE` (1.00) / `MEMRA_SHED_HARVEST` (0.90) of the SLO, and when a starvation sentinel fires. Interactive decode rows sort first; dark prefill runs after decode inside SLO headroom only. This is also the multi-tenant REQUEST-latency dial: fleet QoS@8 measured p50/p95 = 2.39/3.69s at 50ms, 2.06/3.56s at 35ms, 1.64/2.16s at 25ms (bulk pays: 2214 → 1592 → 818 tok/s — `research/qos-p95-20260802/`) |
| `MEMRA_SHED_JUDGE` / `MEMRA_SHED_HARVEST` | 1.00 / 0.90 | per-lane shed thresholds as fractions of the SLO |
| `MEMRA_LANE_MAX_INTERACTIVE`/`_JUDGE`/`_HARVEST` | 32 / 4 / 8 | per-lane resident session caps (interactive's applies to the sidecar shape; the serve path uses `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS`) |
| `MEMRA_PREFILL_TICK` / `MEMRA_PREFILL_JUDGE` / `MEMRA_PREFILL_HARVEST` | 1024 / 256 / 256 | per-tick prefill token budgets: concurrent interactive sessions use 1024 each; a naked sole fresh interactive request widens once to at most 8192 tokens so PP-2 keeps its measured eight-microbatch geometry in one outer prime call. Setting `MEMRA_PREFILL_TICK` explicitly is authoritative and disables that solo widening. Judge/harvest remain per-tick stall bounds (a 2048-tok judge chunk ≈ 230ms decode starvation; 256 ≈ 30ms) further capped by measured SLO headroom |
| `MEMRA_LANE_WINDOW_S` | 30 | interactive step-p99 estimator window |
| `MEMRA_HEALTH_STALL_S` | 120 | how long a BUSY worker may go without a heartbeat before `/health` (== `/livez`) reports 503 (lane/serve-hardening, 2026-08-06). NOT arbitrary: a naked sole fresh interactive request may prime up to 8192 tokens in one call, while concurrent `prefill_tick` work remains capped at `MEMRA_PREFILL_TICK` (1024) tokens per active session and loops every active session inside ONE iteration. At `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS`=64 that concurrent bound is 65,536 primed tokens in one pass ≈ 55 s at the measured 1.2k tok/s 4k-prefill rate; 120 s is that with ~2.2x margin, and the same number `tools/serve-fleet.sh` uses for `LOAD_GRACE`. An IDLE worker is healthy at any beat age (it blocks in `rx.recv()` and legitimately stamps nothing). `/health` publishes `worker.tick_max_ms` — the longest scheduler iteration this process actually observed — so raising this is a measured decision, not a guess. Keep it BELOW `WatchdogSec` in `deploy/systemd/memra-server.service` |
| `MEMRA_WORKER_RESPAWN` | 1 | in-process respawn attempts after the GPU worker thread PANICS, before the process exits 70 for the supervisor to restart it whole. One by default: CUDA errors are sticky per process (after an OOM or an Xid the context is poisoned), so a respawn is a long shot worth exactly one try — it saves a ~120 s weight reload when it works, and a respawn loop against a poisoned context is a box that looks alive and serves nothing. `0` = never retry in-process |
| `MEMRA_GPU_WATCH` | on | `0` disables the GPU-fault watcher (G24): the Xid tail (`/dev/kmsg`, falling back to `journalctl -k -f`; fatal classes 48/64/79/94/95/119/120 latch unhealthy, others count as warnings on `/health`) plus the periodic `nvidia-smi` ECC/row-remap probe. Diagnostics door — leave on in any deployment |
| `MEMRA_GPU_WATCH_S` | 60 | GPU probe interval (seconds) |
| `MEMRA_GPU_PROBE_TIMEOUT_S` | 10 | hard deadline for one `nvidia-smi` probe, and THE alarm for the worst Blackwell wedge class: Xid 119/120 (GSP RPC timeout) emits nothing to the process and hangs the query tools themselves, so a probe that outlives this deadline is treated as a fatal GPU fault rather than waited on. The probe runs as a killed-on-deadline child on its own thread and health reads only atomics, so a hung `nvidia-smi` can never block a health answer |
| `MEMRA_PANIC_AFTER` | unset | **fault injection, never set in a deployment**: panic the GPU worker thread after this many completed requests, once per process. The only way to exercise the G5 supervision ladder (catch_unwind → `/health` 503 with the quoted payload → respawn → exit 70) against a REAL CUDA worker rather than a test fake — which matters because the first version of that supervisor deadlocked startup and only a live gate caught it. Receipts: `research/serve-hardening-20260806/probe-worker-death.sh` + `logs/worker-death.txt`. Costs one relaxed atomic load per completed request when unset |
| `MEMRA_VALLEY_S` | 2.0 | dead-darklane valley threshold (lane/darklane-training, 2026-08-07): how long the worker must be COMPLETELY idle (no active sessions, no queued admissions, no pending HTTP handoffs) before the box counts as "in a valley" and a background job may launch/resume. The signal itself is worker truth already there — `PHASE_IDLE` + heartbeat age + the `PENDING_ADMITS` gauge, zero new hot-path cost — published as `/metrics serve_idle_seconds` (0.0 the instant there is any work). Asymmetric on purpose: yield fires on the busy EDGE with no debounce; only the resume side waits the threshold |
| `MEMRA_BG_JOB` | unset | dead-darklane background job command (`sh -c`, own process group, PDEATHSIG=KILL). Runs ONLY in valleys — a lane class BELOW every serving lane (harvest is still an admitted request class; this is not a request at all). Yield = SIGSTOP to the group on the first sign of traffic, resume = SIGCONT next valley; measured request-fired-to-job-stopped 19.4 ms median / 23.3 ms max (N=5 — one 25 ms poll interval, bound 500 ms). Job exit 0 = complete (never relaunched), exit 75 = checkpointed (relaunches next valley), anything else = failed (never relaunched, loud). Unset = the runner does not exist and `/metrics` has no `bg` block — pre-lane payload byte-identical. Serve impact receipt: `research/darktrain-20260807/` (p95 within noise at the measured shape; see RESULTS) |
| `MEMRA_BG_POLL_MS` | 25 | bg runner poll interval — the detection half of the yield bound (full bound = poll + signal delivery; measured ms-class). Lower = tighter yield, more wakeups; 25 ms is ~1 decode step |
| `MEMRA_BG_YIELD_MODE` | stop | `stop` = SIGSTOP/SIGCONT (the memory — VRAM included — stays resident while stopped; budget for the LIFE of the job). `checkpoint` = the training-class seam: SIGUSR1 means "checkpoint to disk and exit 75", relaunch resumes from the job's own checkpoint next valley (at-least-once: a step may repeat, never be lost). Toy proof: `tools/bg-ckpt-counter.py` |
| `MEMRA_BG_CKPT_GRACE_MS` | 5000 | checkpoint-mode grace: a job still alive this long after SIGUSR1 is SIGKILLed (dirty preempt — resumes from its last on-disk write). The yield bound must hold even against a wedged job |
| `MEMRA_BG_VRAM_MB` | 0 | background job VRAM budget. 0 = CPU-only job, no GPU probe. >0: launch is REFUSED (state `refused_vram`, retried next valley) unless `min free across visible GPUs >= budget + MEMRA_MOE_RESIDENT_HEADROOM_GB` (the same serve headroom the resident planner keeps — the job budget comes out of headroom the SERVER isn't using, never out of the server's own reserve). Min across GPUs, not sum: on a PP-2 pair both cards carry serve shards and the budget must fit the tightest. Fail-closed: unreadable `nvidia-smi` = refusal. The budget is exported to the job's env as `MEMRA_BG_VRAM_MB`; staying inside it at runtime is the JOB's contract (v1 has no cgroup-style VRAM enforcement), and a job that lies gets caught by the same VRAM-aware admission gate that defends serving from everything else |
| `MEMRA_VISION_DIR` | none (text-only) | qwen-class vision tower directory (the checkpoint's `outside.safetensors` with `model.visual.*`). The worker loads the tower at spawn iff this is set, and the HTTP layer accepts `image_url` parts under exactly the same condition; configured-but-unloadable is a boot PANIC (silently serving text-only would be dishonest). ~1.8 GB f32-resident (`memra-server/src/worker.rs`, `main.rs vision_enabled()`) |
| `MEMRA_VISION` | on when `MEMRA_VISION_DIR` set | `0` skips the tower even with the dir configured — the owner knob for VRAM-tight boxes. Image requests then 400 at the HTTP layer |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_VISION` + `MEMRA_GEMMA_MMPROJ` | off | gemma-4 image serving: BOTH are required — `MEMRA_GEMMA_VISION=1` arms the seam and `MEMRA_GEMMA_MMPROJ=<gemma4v mmproj path>` names the tower; unloadable is a boot PANIC. A deployment serves ONE vision family (one model per GPU), so this process-wide switch also decides which placeholder + prep image parts take (`main.rs gemma_vision_enabled()`) |
| `MEMRA_VIDEO_MAX_PIXELS` | 2097152 | serving cap on total video patches (`groups*gh*gw` across ALL sampled frames — the HF smart_resize budget covers `t_bar*h*w`). Deliberately under the HF default because `sdpa_naive` keys the whole span in shared memory (`memra-engine/src/vision_pre.rs video_max_pixels()`). Frame floor/caps are compile-time (`VID_MIN_PIXELS` 4096, `VID_MAX_FRAMES` 32) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC` | unset = K=5 with `MEMRA_DRAFT`, else off | gemma4 SERVED spec depth (`memra-server/src/worker.rs gemma4_spec_k_env()`): unset + a drafter = armed at the shipping K=5; unset without a drafter = off; `0` = the eager/plain kill switch; explicit K>=1 = that depth and REQUIRES `MEMRA_DRAFT` (boot refuses loud). Any other value REFUSES LOUD — the mis-typed-seam law |

---

## 2. Machine-specific config

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_PP_FP8` | off | `1` enables the cuBLASLt FP8-activation prefill GEMM on FP8-native (safetensors) checkpoints via a DUPLICATE resident e4m3 stash kept alongside the Q8_0 slab. +78–129% pp on rtx6000 (2026-07-08); local NV-27B pp1855 1341→1412 (+5.3%, 2026-07-09). Opt-in: costs resident-copy VRAM (`MEMRA_PP_FP8_BUDGET_MB`). **Largely superseded for the per-tensor class** since `MEMRA_ST_E4M3` went default-on (2026-08-05): those tensors never surface as Q8_0, so the stash arm never fires and prefill rides the single resident copy with no budget. Still the route for the block-128 class stash |
| `MEMRA_PP_FP8_BUDGET_MB` | 1536 | VRAM budget for the DUPLICATE resident FP8 weight copies the `MEMRA_PP_FP8` / `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ` stash makes (rig-dependent; 2560 = +16.6% pp1845 on 24GB, 2026-07-08). Irrelevant for the per-tensor class under the default `MEMRA_ST_E4M3` (one copy, no stash — measured: native residency is 430 MiB *smaller* than the slab it replaces, not larger) |
| `MEMRA_ST_E4M3` | **on** | F8-E4M3-origin safetensors projections keep RAW e4m3 as the ONE resident copy (no Q8_0 re-encode): decode dequants e4m3 in-kernel (`qmatvec_e4m3_mmvq` + the batched/fused twins), prefill rides the FP8 GEMM on the same bytes. NEW NUMERIC CONFIG (checkpoint-native weight precision — the Q8_0 hop was the lossy one). `0` = rollback seam to the Q8_0 slab. **DEFAULT-FLIPPED 2026-08-05** (lane/fp8-decode-v1, receipts `research/fp8dec-20260805/`) on the 27B FP8-ST: decode **+2.58%** (38.75 → 39.75 tok/s, N=5 interleaved in one lock hold on one frozen binary, min(B) > max(A) so the distributions do not overlap) — split by the `MEMRA_E4M3_DUAL=0` arm into +1.47pp from the BYTES and +1.09pp from launch fusion; **430 MiB freed** at a measured byte ratio of exactly 1.06250 vs theory 34/32 = 1.06250 (matching to five decimals is itself the proof of single residency — contrast `MEMRA_PP_FP8`, whose stash keeps e4m3 *on top of* the slab and therefore needs a budget); teacher-forced exactness 2/128 near-tie flips with **lower** NLL on the slab arm's own greedy tape than the slab arm scores on it (0.220117 vs 0.222145); kernel-check ALL GREEN incl. 24 E4M3-FUSED cells; run-spec K=1..8 8/8 PASS; serve-st-gate 0 failed (CLI-vs-server ids identical). SCOPE (**widened 2026-08-05**, lane/fp8-blk128-decode): this is now the shared rollback seam for BOTH e4m3 scale classes — the per-tensor scalar class AND the **block-128** class (`MEMRA_ST_E4M3_BLK` is the narrow per-class seam for single-variable A/B). Per-row F8 still takes the Q8_0 re-encode, and GGUF is untouched (`find_fp8_native` is None for GGUF sources) |
| `MEMRA_ST_E4M3_BLK` | **on** | Narrow per-class seam for the BLOCK-128 e4m3 residency arm (lane/fp8-blk128-decode 2026-08-05, receipts `research/fp8blk-20260805/`). `0` sends only the block-128 tensors back to the ARM B' Q8_0 slab while the per-tensor class keeps its shipped default — which is what makes a block-128 A/B single-variable. `MEMRA_ST_E4M3=0` reverts BOTH classes (the doctrine's one seam for all native e4m3 residency). WHY THE CLASS MATTERS: Qwen3.6-FP8 ships `weight_block_size [128,128]`, so before this arm the flagship FP8 class missed the whole native win — the 27B block-128 artifact has 208 such projections (6880 MiB). MEASURED, 27B, N=5 interleaved in ONE lock hold on ONE md5-pinned binary: decode **+1.69%**, **430 MiB** freed (native e4m3 1.0 B/w vs the slab's 1.0625), prefill **+0.83%** over the floor once the class routes through `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ` (a dequant-per-call prefill cost −13.7% before the kernel was vectorized and −5.8% after; ~4.5pp of that was structural 3x-weight-traffic, removable only by the route change). Exactness: branch-(b) (in-kernel per-k128 dequant is not the Q8_0 re-encode's arithmetic, so bit-identity is the wrong bar) — teacher-forced 2/128 near-tie flips with a **reverse-tape control** proving the NLL ordering is a tape artifact (each arm scores best on its own tape: 2/128 @ 0.205029 vs 0/128 @ 0.198189, symmetric); kernel-check ALL GREEN incl. 2 new ragged `[5x128]`/`[6x160]` tail cells; run-spec K=1..8 8/8 PASS |
| `MEMRA_STEP_EP` | off | Step-3.7 live EP correctness seam, formatted `LAYER[-LAYER]@DEVICE,DEVICE[;...]` (for example `24-25@1,2;31@0,2`). Every selected unclamped routed-expert layer drops its single-device expert slab, assigns each native block-E4M3 expert to exactly one of 2–8 distinct RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell devices, executes real top-8 routes on the owner ranks, returns the deterministic routed sum through host memory, and keeps router/shared-expert work on the owning PP stage. Duplicate layer assignments, unregistered models, clamped Step layers, non-native expert banks, non-PRO hardware, duplicate devices, and a device list that omits a layer's PP owner refuse before CUDA. Expert dispatch/combine remains host-bounce correctness only (`native_p2p=false`, `performance_claim=false`) until native EP transport earns a separate gate. Unset, empty, or literal `0` disables the seam. This is selected-layer serving bring-up, not complete-model EP or product throughput. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP` | off | Step-3.7 live TP correctness seam. Explicit placement uses `LAYER[-LAYER]@DEVICE,DEVICE[;...]`; `all@0,1,2,3` selects the complete official TP4 trunk (layers 0–44). PP or mixed-owner layouts keep the explicit form so each list can begin with that layer's owning stage device. Before the first model weight upload, one fail-closed preflight validates every selected layer's PP owner, attention shards, expert layout, unique runtime group, uniform PRO hardware, and the official block-128 FP8 expert census. The loader snapshots the parsed TP/EP specs and strict transport/arithmetic flags once; every layer executes from that same immutable configuration rather than re-reading process state. Q/K/V and canonical O reduction use real tensor-parallel rank ownership. With native P2P, eager decode, replay-free verification, and the separately gated per-session multi-token prefill keep QK norm, RoPE, KV/cache, and attention rank-local on all 45 registered layers. The 12 global layers use context-linear sharded caches; the 33 SWA layers use one shared absolute-position ring plan over independent per-rank q8_0/q5_1 planes, physically capped at `min(max_ctx, 512 + 4096 + 31)` rows. Hydration copies only the owning cache's aligned live prefix; append/rebase preserves the active transaction's rollback window; attention reads one contiguous last-window view; growth copies only the checkpoint-visible live prefix and preserves transaction generation. Flat-history prefix snapshots, restores, and fanout refuse once either the owner or distributed SWA ring exists. Without native P2P, SWA and global attention retain the owning-stage fallback. Server admission reserves every unmaterialized distributed-KV sidecar on its owning rank, charging full context only for global layers and the bounded physical ring for SWA, then drops that logical reservation layer-by-layer once live CUDA allocation accounts for it. Routed experts preserve the checkpoint's 128x128 E4M3 blocks: TP2 tensor-shards their 1280-wide projections, while TP4 uses expert ownership because 1280/4 cuts those scale blocks. Router, head gate, and replicated shared-expert work stay on the owning PP stage. One `MEMRA_STEP_TP` spec selects the complete per-layer hybrid; operators do not also set `MEMRA_STEP_EP`. Illegal Step variants, head geometry, scale-block cuts, clamped layers, non-PRO hardware, duplicate devices/layers, conflicting EP/TP assignments, or a rank list whose first device is not the PP owner refuse before execution. Every marker reports attention TP and expert EP separately; this is TP4 serving bring-up, not pipeline parallelism, independent replicas, or a TP8 hardware claim. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P` | off | `1` enables correctness-first `cuMemcpyPeerAsync` collectives for Step TP. Startup requires directed peer capability, peer and default-pool access, and an exact 16 KiB / 64 KiB / 1 MiB / 64 MiB size-class ladder in every direction. TP attention projections/cache/O, the TP2 expert program, and TP4/TP8 expert-owner dispatch/return use native peer copies after qualification. Expert activation and route accumulation stay host-canonical unless the separately gated device-arithmetic path is enabled, and every marker labels the choice. Any other nonempty value refuses. This is an opt-in exactness gate with `performance_claim=false`, not a throughput-qualified default. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_BULK_P2P` | off | Strict `0`/`1` experimental transport shape for Step TP; `1` requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P=1`. It replaces token-row Q/K/V gathers with one dense peer transfer per remote rank plus byte-preserving root placement, replaces token-row O scatters with byte-preserving root packing plus one dense transfer per remote TP8 block, and assembles fixed-row F32 projection chunks with one byte-preserving strided placement kernel instead of one D2D copy per token. Projection kernels and the canonical eight-block O reduction order are unchanged. Official CUDA 13.1/12.8 raw-bit gates passed for both transport changes. On the exact 16K+512 c=1 agentic fixture, they reduced TTFT from `113.252s` to `23.487s` and raised throughput from `3.691` to `10.321 tok/s`; the 16K prefill profile reduced peer copies `61,452 -> about 21` per 4K attention layer and D2D APIs `17,697,224 -> 2,504` across four chunks. The current experimental stage additionally keeps the large gathered-attention, canonical O, and K/V shadow handoffs on the root device instead of round-tripping them through host memory; positions, head-gate control, MoE routes, and final logits remain host-visible. This newest stage still requires official dual-CUDA equality, structural D2H collapse, and high-context serving proof. Default remains off. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_EP_DEVICE_ARITHMETIC` | off | `1` keeps TP4/TP8 expert-owner SwiGLU activation and slot-ordered route accumulation on the GPUs after native peer dispatch. It requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P=1` and a `MEMRA_STEP_TP` TP4/TP8 expert-owner layer; explicit legacy EP, host-bounce, and TP2 projection-sharded experts refuse. The owner uses Memra's host-matching `expf` transcription plus explicit round-to-nearest operations; rank 0 accumulates in the existing token/slot order and performs one final readback. Unset or `0` preserves the host-canonical rollback. This is an opt-in raw-bit exactness path with `performance_claim=false`, not a throughput-qualified default. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_F32_MIRROR` | off | `1` expands each Step TP Q/K/V/O BF16 shard to exact F32 once during model load, releases that shard's BF16 TP copy, and reuses the resident F32 values with the same TP1/TP2/TP4/TP8 canonical chunk shapes. It requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP`; unset or `0` preserves BF16 residency with decode-time expansion, and any other value refuses. The owning-stage fallback weights are unchanged. This is an opt-in measured decode optimization that trades additional rank VRAM for fewer allocations and conversion kernels; it is not enabled by native P2P or device expert arithmetic automatically. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_PREFILL` | off | Strict `0`/`1` Step-3.7 multi-token prefill door. **TP2 ADMITTED 2026-08-25, UNQUALIFIED:** the shape check named `ranks == 4` while the prefill body is rank-count-generic, so a 2-card native-P2P placement could never open the door even though its O reduction is rank-local. TP4's high-context NO-GO below is a TRANSPORT verdict about that placement's peer-copy count, not evidence about TP2. TP2 carries NO receipts yet — prefill argmax identity and a TTFT measurement are owed before any claim, and the door stays off by default. `1` requires the qualified TP4 placement, native P2P, device arithmetic, and non-FP8 KV; it keeps Q/K/V, QK norm, RoPE, quantized KV append, attention, and canonical O reduction rank-local across all 45 trunk layers. Official layers 3 and clamped 43, 24+29-token continuation, rollback after a real partial commit, retry, and handoff into eager TP4 decode are raw-bit exact on CUDA 13.1 and 12.8. Cross-request concat-prime did not clear its live-server performance gate, so a Step prime-batch call with this door open refuses before cache mutation; the server restores the prompt queues and serves each request through the qualified per-session path. **HIGH-CONTEXT PERFORMANCE NO-GO (2026-08-18):** on one four-card RTX PRO 6000 host with the official `b3d7916f` FP8 checkpoint and an exact 16K+512 coding-agent fixture, Memra c=1 measured `3.6912 tok/s`, `113.252s` TTFT, and `138.707s` wall versus best-valid vLLM 0.27.1 Triton+MTP3 at `139.433 tok/s`, `1.099s`, and `3.672s`. An unprofiled four-chunk gate reproduced `115.554s` inside prefill itself. A partial Nsight trace and the source agree on the wall: three token-row column gathers plus six remote O blocks issue about `61,440` peer copies and matching event waits per 4K attention layer. Do not claim TP4 P2P throughput or enable this door by default; the next gate is a persistent bulk pack/transpose/peer-copy/unpack collective that preserves canonical arithmetic. Default remains off. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_EP_GROUPED_PREFILL` | off | Strict `0`/`1` Step-3.7 routed-expert prefill door. `1` requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP_PREFILL=1`, native P2P, device arithmetic, and the official TP4 expert-owner layout. One model-scoped capacity plan is reused across all 42 routed layers for 16–4096 active tokens, including capacity-backed slabs whose active prefix is shorter than the allocation; Step supplies only layer clamp policy and tensor views. Official 24+29-token prefill is raw-bit exact on CUDA 13.1 and 12.8, cuts the balanced N=5 median from `1.50207s` to `0.57160s` (`2.6278x`), and leaves peak HBM unchanged. A 24/29-token cross-request B=2 concat implementation was also exact, but its balanced live-server N=5 median improved only `0.953301s` to `0.925863s` (`1.029636x`), below the declared `1.20x` retention bar; that execution path was removed. Concurrent requests therefore use the scheduler's per-session grouped fallback. Default remains off. |
| `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ_X8` | **on for `m<=8`** | Literal `0` rolls short decode/verify calls back to the prior 128/256-token padded launch; unset or `1` uses Memra's own 128-output-row x 8-token tactic. The kernel keeps the existing dynamic `1x128` E4M3 activation quantizer, block-128 E4M3 weights/scales, MMA instruction, and epilogue; it only stops computing padded token columns. Exactness: full ragged battery plus every Step expert width `m=1..8` passed on RTX 5090, and `m=1/4/8` passed independently on all three RTX PRO 6000 cards (zero EXACT bit mismatches; full 254-code E4M3 coverage). Balanced adjacent N=6 on 5090 measured **2.72-3.91x** the old FP8 launch and **2.07-2.91x** Q8; balanced N=5 per arm per card on PRO measured **2.17-3.52x** the old FP8 launch and **1.75-2.64x** Q8 across Step gate/up/down shapes. These are synchronized GEMM-kernel ratios, not end-to-end Step throughput. External implementations informed the operand-orientation hypothesis only; shipped CUDA and dispatch are Memra-owned and have no external header, submodule, or runtime dependency. |
| `MEMRA_FP8_FOLD` | off | ARM A (lane fp8-gemm-arm 2026-08-03). `1` = fold a block-128 FP8 scale grid into ONE per-tensor scale at load (global-amax re-encode, `s = amax/448`) so the per-tensor e4m3 consumers (`MEMRA_ST_E4M3` resident arm + `try_fp8_gemm`) take a Qwen-official / DeepSeek-lineage block-128 checkpoint unchanged. LOSSY where block dynamic range varies — that is the arm's question, not a free win |
| `MEMRA_FP8_BLK_GPU` | off | ARM B' (lane fp8-gemm-arm 2026-08-03). `1` = block-128 FP8 weights dequant to Q8_0 **on the GPU** at load (`cu/fp8_blk_dequant.cu`) instead of host dequant + host re-encode. BYTE-IDENTICAL to the CPU path (kernel-check `fp8-blk-gpu`: 0 mismatching bytes on aligned + both ragged edges; real-checkpoint 4-prompt argmax MATCH with bit-equal top-5 logits), so decode and dispatch are unchanged — this is load wall only: 35.65s → 9.22s (3.87x, N=3 interleaved) on a 2.65 GB block-128 checkpoint. Unlike ARM A it keeps every block's own scale (Q8_0's per-32 grid is finer than the source 128×128). Opt-in until the 27B-class rig gates run; CPU path stays default |
| `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ` | **on** for the native-resident source, off for the stash | **TWO OPERAND SOURCES, TWO DEFAULTS since lane/fp8-blk128-decode (2026-08-05).** Unset = the checkpoint-native `QT_F8_E4M3_BLK` residency grid takes this tile (ON); `0` reverts that route to dequant-per-call; `1` additionally admits the load-time e4m3 STASH source (a duplicate copy next to a Q8_0 slab, spending `MEMRA_PP_FP8_BUDGET_MB`), which stays opt-in. Same tile, different defaults, because the floors differ — see DIFFERENT DENOMINATOR below. The split matters mechanically, not just editorially: the stash gate is also what admits the duplicate at LOAD time (`model.rs`), so one shared gate would have made the native flip silently duplicate every Q8_0 tensor with an fp8 sibling. P1 option (b) (lane/fp8-mmq 2026-08-04). `1` = block-128 FP8 prefill GEMMs run through memra's OWN per-block MMQ tile (`cu/mmq_fp8_blk.cu`) instead of falling to the Q8_0 floor. The checkpoint's e4m3 bytes ARE the MMA A operand (`m16n8k32` e4m3×e4m3; **the form is `kind::mxf8f6f4.block_scale.scale_vec::1X` at the ue8m0 identity scale since 2026-08-06, a 309-TF class at 16.06 cyc/warp-MMA — it shipped on plain `kind::f8f6f4`, which is a 155-TF class at 32.02 cyc, NOT the 381-TF class this line used to claim; see `MEMRA_MMQ_FP8BLK_PLAIN`. Every "not MMA-bound against 381" reading below was taken against a ceiling this kernel never had — the measured TFLOP numbers are real, the ceiling was 155**) and each `[128×128]` block keeps its own f32 scale, so unlike ARM A (`MEMRA_FP8_FOLD`) there is NO weight-side re-quantization and unlike ARM B' (`MEMRA_FP8_BLK_GPU`) it is not stuck at Q8_0-MMQ throughput. Exactness gate: `fp8_mmq_check` EXACT arm is BIT-IDENTICAL (0 differing bits, 9 shapes incl. ragged n/k) to a host reference of the same arithmetic, with a committed negative control. Admits TWO operand sources (2026-08-05): the block-128 raw-bytes+grid load **stash** (a second e4m3 copy next to a Q8_0 slab, shares `MEMRA_PP_FP8_BUDGET_MB`) and, since lane/fp8-blk128-decode, the **resident** `blk` grid on a `QT_F8_E4M3_BLK` tensor — the checkpoint-native single copy, no slab and no budget. Same bytes, same grid, same layout contract, so the kernel cannot tell them apart. That second source is what makes it the block-128 class's prefill route: `try_e4m3_blk_prefill` calls it first and falls through to the dequant only on refusal. Refuses any tensor containing an e4m3 NaN code (hardware reads 0x7F as NaN, the host/ARM B' convention as 0.0) — scanned once per tensor, not assumed. **v2 verdict (lane/fp8-mmq-v2 2026-08-04, receipts `research/fp8st-20260804/mmq-v2/LANE-VERDICT.jsonl`): stays default OFF.** v2 restructured the mainloop so the 128-wide scale block is the outer k loop and one chained unscaled accumulator folds `s_blk*dB` once per block (activation scale per-128, tile 61KB→37KB), which moved GEMM-only from v1's uniform 0.81-0.94x floor to parity-class at long context: 27B m=6257 q 1.006 / o 0.989 / gate_up 0.993 / down 0.994 / square 1.023, and 27B m=512 down_proj 1.089x. It does NOT reach ~1.1x, and local end-to-end pp on the 1.7B is 0.9899x (pp512) / 0.9951x (pp6257) because that model's narrow projections measure 0.846-0.894x at m=512. Model-level drift improved ~130x (perplexity +0.11% → +0.0008%) with 3/128 teacher-forced near-tie flips and 2/192 in the 6-draw loop. Occupancy (halved `MMQ_Y`, `minBlocksPerMultiprocessor=2`) and cp.async weight double-buffering were both implemented and REFUTED — their build seams (`MEMRA_MMQ_Y_FP8`/`MEMRA_MMQ_OCC_FP8`/`MEMRA_MMQ_PIPE_FP8`) and dispatch arms were DELETED per the flags doctrine (v0.69.0); the lane rows are the record — re-read them before re-proposing either. Token-tile width is chosen per call by wave fill, not by `out_f` (an out_f threshold mis-picked every 1.7B projection). **DIFFERENT DENOMINATOR ON THE NATIVE-RESIDENT CLASS (lane/fp8-blk128-decode 2026-08-05):** v2's 0.85–1.09x was measured against a floor whose Q8_0 slab was ALREADY RESIDENT — tile vs tile. On the block-128 residency arm the floor must also CREATE that slab every prefill call (27.9 ms/pass measured after the dequant kernel was vectorized), so the tile only has to not be 27.9 ms worse than a kernel it trails by ≤15% on a subset of shapes. Measured 3-arm interleaved N=3 on the 27B: slab 1540.5 / blk-dequant 1449.1 / **blk-MMQ 1553.3** tok/s = 1.0083x the floor with min(C) > max(A), ledger 832 dispatches. Same tile, opposite sign, because the question changed. **DEFAULT FLIPPED for that source (2026-08-05)**, on the exactness cells 6b741068 deferred it on — measured on `prime_cache`, the class that actually dispatches this kernel, with a ledger on every arm (624 = 208 projections x 3 passes = full coverage) and an A==B bit-identical control proving the instrument can see zero where zero is: argmax UNCHANGED and top-10 order identical to the floor's, rms_rel 2.5e-2 on an rms-2.7155 logit vector, teacher-forced NLL on the prompt's own continuation LOWER than the floor's (2.764722 vs 2.787267 = −0.81%) on a tape neither arm produced, +2/511 argmax disagreements against a 50.29% floor baseline = the near-tie class; run-spec K=1..8 8/8 PASS, serve-st-gate + serve-smoke 0 failed, kernel-check ALL GREEN. Post-flip re-measured NAKED (`postflip-*` receipts): the new default dispatches 1040 and reproduces 1553.9 tok/s, `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ=0` dispatches 0 and reproduces the dequant arm's 1449.1 exactly — the seam is real, not nominal. The +0.83% margin was then re-settled on its own two-arm sweep with the arms ADJACENT and the order ALTERNATING (`pairsweep.sh`, N=6 pairs), because a third arm sitting between the two being compared breaks the interleaving law for that pair: N median 1553.0 vs floor 1540.6, min(N) 1548.7 > max(floor) 1541.2 = DISJOINT, 6/6 pairwise wins, mean +12.72 tok/s = **+0.83%** — the pre-flip figure reproduced. The dequant arm is NOT dead code: it is where every tile precondition (in_f % 16, grid dims, scale == 1.0, the e4m3-NaN scan) still falls through, so a checkpoint the tile refuses keeps exact prefill instead of losing the class. |
| `MEMRA_KV_K` | `q8_0` | K-cache format arm. `fp8` FLIP-BLOCKED (2026-07-09 A/B, tag kvk-fp8-ab-9bst): e2e FLAT (+0.3-0.9% ms/tok — the −7-14% micro didn't survive; micro≠e2e) AND 9B ST spec self-consistency FAIL (acceptance 74%→20.5%, drift accumulates across K reads). q8_0 stays |
| `MEMRA_KV_V` | `q5_1` | V-cache format arm: `q4_0` FLAT + quality-taxed (argmax MISMATCH in-config), `fp8` borderline (−2pt) — measured 2026-07-08; q5_1 stays |
| `MEMRA_SWA_RING` | off | Experimental Step35 owning-stage bounded KV ring (`1` enables; lane/kv256-capacity). This flag controls the legacy owner cache and MTP scratch; native rank-local TP SWA uses its own bounded ring contract under `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P`. SWA trunk layers and the SWA MTP scratch allocate `min(max_ctx, 512 + 4096 + 31) = min(max_ctx, 4639)` physical rows while full-attention layers retain `max_ctx`; Gemma4 and every non-Step35 architecture are unchanged.
