| flag | binary | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_DC_GATE=N` | gemma-gate | device-counter decode gate: the dc chain's N-token greedy stream must be IDENTICAL to the eager `decode_step` chain; prints both throughputs (default N=64) |
| `MEMRA_VERIFY_GATE=K` | gemma-gate | batched-verify self-consistency (the base of the documented `MEMRA_VERIFY_GATE2`): tokenwise reference, then ONE `decode_step_t` over the last K tokens — per-position argmax must match (default K=4) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC=K` (+`MEMRA_DRAFT`) | gemma-gate, gemma-spec-session-gate | MTP spec loop cell: prime, draft K, verify, accept; compares the spec stream against plain greedy + times both (default K=4; the FR-rank corpus mint reads it too, default 7 there). Distinct from the serving flag `MEMRA_SPEC_K` |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_ONLY=1` | gemma-gate (dflash cell) | skip the plain-greedy reference arm (timing-only run) |
| `MEMRA_GEN_CORPUS=<prompt dir>` + `MEMRA_GEN_OUT=<ids.txt>` | gemma-gate | FR-rank corpus mint: one model load, run the spec loop over every `*.txt` prompt, append each generation's token ids one line per prompt. Needs `MEMRA_DRAFT`; setting these together with `MEMRA_SPEC_DFLASH` REFUSES (the two select different gemma-gate runs and the mint branch would win silently — the 2026-08-16 "summary never emitted" incident) |

| `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ_NLL=<txt>` | fp8-mmq-stream | mean token NLL over a real text window (one prefill) — the quality-sanity cell |
| `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ_TF=<log>` | fp8-mmq-stream | teacher-force from a prior run's `stream ids: [...]` line (cross-arm identical trajectory) |
| `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ_LOGITS=<file>` | fp8-mmq-stream | dump the logit rows raw for cross-arm drift comparison |
| `MEMRA_OPTI_PROMPT` / `MEMRA_OPTI_CAP` / `MEMRA_OPTI_STRESS_NGEN` | optipipe-gate | real prompt text (chat-rendered under `MEMRA_CHAT`; default numeric ids 101..229) / session cache capacity (REFUSES below the gate's required minimum) / alternate-mode generation budget (default 256) |
| `MEMRA_PPN_GATE_WINDOW` | ppn-gate | pipelined-arm tokens-in-flight cap (default 3; `1` = drain every step) — bisect seam, not a config |
| `MEMRA_PROBE_DEVICE` | spec-st-probe, vision-gate, spec-serve-gate | CUDA device ordinal for the probe engine (default 0) |
| `MEMRA_PROBE_CTX` | spec-st-probe | session cache capacity — reproduce the worker's `MEMRA_CTX`-floor allocation (scratch.cap feeds `fa_decode_dc`'s n_splits geometry), default prompt+gen+64 |
| `MEMRA_REPLAY_K` / `MEMRA_REPLAY_STRIDE` / `MEMRA_REPLAY_CHUNK` / `MEMRA_REPLAY_T` | replay-acceptance | draft depth (4) / positions between draft chains (16) / prefill chunk (512) / token cap (65536 in directory mode, 0 = whole file in single-file mode). Needs `MEMRA_PROMPT_FILE` (file OR directory) |
| `MEMRA_REPLAY_HDUMP=<dir>` | replay-acceptance | hidden-state dumps (`<stem>.hiddens` + `.meta.json`) — REQUIRED in directory mode (bulk distillation extraction; skip draft chains there via a huge STRIDE) |
| `MEMRA_REPLAY_DUMP=<file>` | replay-acceptance | per-position draft/target/hit JSONL dump |
| `MEMRA_REPLAY_GATE` (set) | replay-acceptance | chunk-offset exactness gate: re-run at chunk 64 — the greedy track must be EXACT; draft slots allow <=1% disagreement (chunk-FP-order near-ties, measured 2/127) |
| `MEMRA_ST_NGEN=N` | run-safetensors | greedy continuation gate (native-vs-GGUF byte compare), default 32 |
| `MEMRA_ORACLE_OUT=<file>` | run-safetensors | Write the native checkpoint oracle TSV used by `memra model verify checkpoint`; the onboarding CLI sets this for its pinned native runner. Gate output path, not serving config. |
| `MEMRA_ARTIFACT_LOCK=<file>` / `MEMRA_REWRITE_RECEIPT=<file>` | rewrite gate binaries | Bind a generated rewrite receipt to the exact artifact lock and select the output path. Qualification refuses to emit a receipt without the lock; these are gate-harness inputs, not serving knobs. |
| `MEMRA_NATIVE_CHECKPOINT_RUNNER=<binary>` / `MEMRA_NATIVE_SERVE_RUNNER=<binary>` | memra model verify | Environment alternatives to `--native-runner` for checkpoint and serve qualification. There is no compatibility fallback when the runner is absent or fails. |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_VERIFY_TIMEOUT_S=N` | memra model verify serve | Readiness deadline for the temporary native server, default 180 seconds. Timeout is a failed serve gate and never promotes support. |

| `MEMRA_STEP_EP_DYNAMIC_CAPACITY_TOKENS` / `MEMRA_STEP_EP_NUMERIC_ITERS` | ep-step-fp8-gate | Dynamic owner-grouped workspace capacity (default initial token count + 2) and numeric probe repetitions (default 50, must be positive). |
| `MEMRA_TP_DEVICES` / `MEMRA_TP_TOKENS` / `MEMRA_TP_REPS` | Step TP/EP gates | Comma-separated gate device ordinals (normally `0,1`; allowed rank count depends on the gate), test token count (gate-specific default 1, 2, or 3), and interleave count for `tp-step-resident-device-gate` (default 5 — host-arm/device-arm alternation per rep, the box-drift protocol). These select fixture topology and gate repetition only. |
| `MEMRA_TP_GATE_GROW` / `MEMRA_TP_GATE_SWA` | tp-step-attention-gate | `1` exercises transaction growth or the official sliding-window layer; invalid values and an SWA request on a non-window layer fail closed. |
| `MEMRA_CPU_FILE_IDENTITY_KEEP=<path>` | cpu-native-check | persistent fixture path (written only if absent, never removed) so shm-cache warm restarts can be exercised across processes; default is an unlinked per-process temp file |
| `MEMRA_CPU_NATIVE_BENCH_QTYPE` / `MEMRA_CPU_NATIVE_BENCH_THREADS` | cpu-native-check (under `MEMRA_CPU_NATIVE_BENCH`) | bench quant type (`Q8_0/Q4_0/Q2_K/Q3_K/Q4_K/Q5_K/Q6_K/IQ4_XS...`, default Q2_K) / OMP threads (default 8) |
| `MEMRA_GV_CAUSAL=1` | gemma-vision-e2e | run the WRONG arm end-to-end (sets `MEMRA_GV_FORCE_CAUSAL=1`): overlay rows spliced but plain causal mask — the ship-by-analogy bug, kept as the probe's negative control |
| `MEMRA_LOCKSTEP_M` | run-lockstep | lockstep stream count (`1..=16`, default 2) |
| `MEMRA_INVALID_PINNED_FRAC_TEST_CHILD` | cargo test (spill.rs) | test-internal self-exec marker for the invalid-pinned-frac child-process test; never set by hand |
| `MEMRA_PROMPTS_FILE=<file>` | run-lockstep | one prompt per line, one per stream — the mixed-workload regime (same-prompt streams route identically and overstate expert overlap). Default: `MEMRA_PROMPT` for every stream (the identity-gate regime) |
| `MEMRA_PROMPT_DIR=<dir>` | run-spec | BENCH-SWEEP mode: iterate every `*.txt` prompt in the dir with ONE model load |
| `MEMRA_BENCH_QT` | gu-wall-bench | expert-quant candidate: `iq3s` (default) \| `iq4xs` \| `nvfp4` |
| `MEMRA_SG_SEEDS` / `MEMRA_SG_TOKENS` / `MEMRA_SG_TEMP` / `MEMRA_SG_TOPK` / `MEMRA_SG_TOPP` / `MEMRA_SG_REP` / `MEMRA_SG_FREQ` / `MEMRA_SG_PRESENT` / `MEMRA_SG_LASTN` / `MEMRA_SG_PROMPT` | dspark_sample_gate | hist-mode distribution gate for the dspark route's SAMPLED admission (lane/dspark-sampled-admission-20260820): seeds per arm (2000) / positions compared (4) / temperature (0.8) / top-k (0) / top-p (1.0) / repetition penalty (1.0 = off) / frequency penalty (0.0) / presence penalty (0.0) / penalty window (8,192 tokens, the serve API's cross-path bound; lane/dspark-penalized-sampled-20260821 — with a penalty set BOTH arms carry it, the plain arm through the same boundary-sampler penalty pass, so hist gates penalized spec against penalized trunk-only) / comma-separated prompt token ids (default a fixed 24-id prompt). Spec-on vs trunk-only per-position two-sample chi-square; the tiny mode takes no knobs; t0 additionally gates the penalties-at-T=0 refusal |
| `MEMRA_NMEASURE_REPS` | run-gen | steady-state benchmark repetitions (default 1, min 1) |

## 5. Experimental doors (opt-in, documented block)

> **DFlash2 qualification boundary:** The 2026-08-24 RTX PRO 6000 LOW/HIGH
> throughput and demotion ladder below is **greedy only**. Historical gates prove route
> correctness, but ordinary omitted-temperature serving is sampled and may carry vendor
> penalties; that c1-to-saturation DFlash2 path remains unqualified, and sampled sessions
> intentionally do not demote. While one is live, it also blocks a later greedy request from
> widening through LOW: otherwise the pair would serialize two DFlash bursts below HIGH. The
> route remains default-OFF and must not be flipped in production until a current-head
> served-default sampled-plus-penalty ladder is green.

| flag | door | block |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_SPEC=1` + `MEMRA_DSPARK_DRAFT=<export_dir>` | dspark drafter spec route for the qwen-hybrid trunk (lane/dspark-q38-recover): one `DflashDraft` per non-gemma model, `DsparkSpecSession` bursts on the gemma-route policy shape — greedy (argmax verify, unpenalized — penalized greedy stays on the plain path and the engine refuses temp==0+penalties by name) OR sampled (temperature>0 rides the route's rejection-sampling verify, lane/dspark-sampled-admission-20260820; penalties INCLUDED since lane/dspark-penalized-sampled-20260821 — the accept walk penalizes the verify columns over the true per-state window, within-round accepts included), unconstrained, text-only, COLD sessions only; GREEDY admission takes the spec-gate LOW band wave-projected (`projected_admission_wave <= LOW` — this request + arriving wave + HTTP in-flight; a wave never admits a drafting session it is about to demote) and a live greedy session DEMOTES to batched plain decode at `n_live >= HIGH` via `DsparkSpecSession::into_demoted` (byte-identity gate: plain == dspark == dspark+MEMRA_SPEC_DEMOTE_AT, 16/16, PRO 6000 2026-08-24, darklanes research/dflash2-pro6000-20260824; ladder receipts: c=1 +1.7%, warm c=4/c=8 at plain parity 273.8/429 vs the pre-lane -37% c=8 solo-burst drag); SAMPLED sessions share the greedy LOW band since lane/dspark-sampled-wave-20260825, and since lane/dspark-low-band-stack-20260825 dspark rows STACK inside that band (wave-projection-bounded at LOW, the same bound MTP's sampled admission has; sampled rows still never demote — session-owned philox; `MEMRA_DSPARK_SAMPLED_WAVE=0` restores the pre-stack posture: solo sampled AND the one-row block; see the `MEMRA_DSPARK_SAMPLED_WAVE` row), and `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE=0` or an operator `MEMRA_SPEC_K>0` pin restores solo-only admission alongside the no-automatic-demotion policy; `MEMRA_SPEC_K=0` pins plain and refuses DFlash2 admission entirely. Arming it DISABLES the MTP spec arm for that model and the server refuses un-co-gated combinations at boot (`MEMRA_SPEC_DFLASH`, `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC`, `MEMRA_PP_STAGES>1`, armed nonzero `MEMRA_STEP_TP`, or armed nonzero `MEMRA_STEP_EP`) — two spec programs on one model must never silently coexist. Unset = route absent, zero served-path change | Runtime default OFF: the server never auto-attaches a DFlash2 artifact — arming is the operator's explicit opt-in. It is, however, the qualified SERVING route for Qwen3.8-27B on both measured card classes: on RTX PRO 6000 it beats the MTP arm on every rung of the vendor-default sampled shape (c1 127/117, c2 128/120, c4 87/85 agg tok/s, x3 interleaved, darklanes research/dflash2-pro6000-20260824/stack-cell/) and has carried both production q38 origins since 2026-08-25 (v0.113.0, deploy gates with sampled speculation receipts); on RTX 5090 the q4 + target-head-rank recipe is the measured opt-in (+3.87% E2E). Per-card recipes in `docs/COOKBOOK.md`. That model/hardware evidence does not transfer to other GPUs. The older box-2 cum1000 1.00x own-traffic / 1.11x math result remains historical evidence for the untrimmed recover-era artifact, not a prohibition on the newer 5090 recipe. |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_SAMPLED_WAVE` | on | Sampled DFlash2 admission takes the spec-gate LOW band wave-projected, exactly like greedy (lane/dspark-sampled-wave-20260825; owner serving law: the vendor-default serve shape IS sampled — under the old solo law the route lost speculation for the fleet's dominant traffic whenever anything else was live, measured on the 2026-08-25 DE flip). Sampled sessions still never demote; since lane/dspark-low-band-stack-20260825 dspark rows STACK inside the LOW band (the one-non-demotable-row block was analogy, refuted by the MTP arm on the same box: two sampled MTP sessions at c=2 = 121.6 vs the blocked 1-spec+1-plain mix's 105.9) — the wave projection bounds the count at LOW, exactly as it bounds MTP's own sampled admission, and greedy rows among them still demote at HIGH. `0` restores the solo law without touching the greedy band. Serving default requires the sampled ladder receipts in darklanes research/dflash2-pro6000-20260824/sampled-wave/ — this row ships in the same PR as the flag, per the flags law. |
| `MEMRA_NONSTREAM_DEADLINE_GATE` | on | Refuse a NON-STREAMING request whose pessimistic estimate cannot finish inside its `timeout_ms` deadline, at admission — 400 `nonstream_deadline_infeasible`, naming the `max_tokens` that WOULD fit and the streaming alternative. Costs no slot, opens no receipt, burns no GPU. `0` lets the request run; on `/v1/completions` and `/v1/chat/completions` it is then covered by partial delivery, but on `/v1/messages` and `/v1/responses` it 408s and discards the work as before (those dialects have no honest partial shape — see SERVING.md), so turning the gate off is strictly worse there. Default ON with receipts (darklanes research/nonstream-deadline-20260826): the owner-reported bug was a 30,278-token non-streaming request answering **408 after 90.7 s with every generated token discarded**, while the SAME work streamed 200 in 93.8 s. Measured ladder at that prompt — 4096 out 52.0 s, 5120 61.9 s, 6144 71.5 s (200); 8192 90.7 s, 16384 91.5 s (408) — and the gate's boundary is pinned to it by test (`the_feasibility_gate_boundary_matches_the_measured_ladder`): allows through 6144, refuses 8192+. Streaming is NEVER gated (its deadline bounds only time-to-first-token). A ctx-bounded request (no `max_tokens`) is never gated either — the owner ruling is that context is then its only limit. Rollback seam: this flag; the partial-delivery half has no flag because discarding a customer's tokens is not a posture worth preserving. |
| `MEMRA_PREFILL_FLOOR_TOK_S` | 2000 | Pessimistic prefill rate for the feasibility estimate ONLY (never billing, never admission accounting). Deliberately below measurement — ~2.9k tok/s observed at 30k tokens on the serving cards — because a tight estimate would refuse requests that succeed, and a false refusal is worse than a slow success. Matches the floor the api-router already uses for its own header budget. |
| `MEMRA_DECODE_FLOOR_TOK_S` | 60 | Pessimistic decode rate for the same estimate. The slowest arm measured through the customer path on the current fleet is ~100 tok/s (verbose prose at 30k context); 60 leaves headroom for a busier box. Together with `DEADLINE_INFEASIBLE_MARGIN_PCT` (150, compile-time) this is what places the refusal boundary between the measured 6144-pass and 8192-fail. |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH2_SDPA_CLIP` | on | `0` restores the LEGACY full-scan windowed SDPA for the DFlash2 round attention (`sdpa_naive_w`: dynamic shared memory sized `T_kv*4` bytes and an O(ctx) key scan) — the rollback seam for the lo-clipped kernel (lane/dflash2-longctx-20260821) and the long-ctx gate's crash-reproduction arm. Default ON uses `sdpa_naive_w_lo`: keys below every query's window floor are never read, shared memory is `(window-1+T)*4` bytes (~8KB at window 2048) and the launch bound that killed the route above ~12k ctx (GATES-SMOKE-20260821 B2: DriverError(CUDA_ERROR_INVALID_VALUE) at ctx 16,571/30,157, last success 9,510) is gone. BYTE-IDENTICAL output (masked keys contribute exact zeros to same-order reductions; kernel_check `sdpa_naive_w_lo` cell pins bit-identity at Tkv 4104 and, as teeth, the legacy launch failure at Tkv 16392), and a measured wall WIN at agent ctx (the O(window) scan: dspark_q38_gate mean spec 123.8 vs 104.1 tok/s on 0.6-8.9k-token real agentic prompts, box NJ 2026-08-21). DFlash2 route only; the cum1000/markov full-attention arm keeps the T_kv*4 bound (a full-attention drafter cannot clip — named residual). Never off in a measured default run |
| `MEMRA_LOCKSTEP_BATCH_ATTN=1` | batched m-band full-attention mixer for multi-stream lockstep decode: q/k/v + output projections run once at m (streams share weights), KV-bound work stays per stream. Bit-identical (same-prompt m=2 token-exact vs the per-stream path) | MEASURED FLAT 2026-07-25: m=2 4.72/4.72, m=3 5.24 vs 5.35. Full-attn is the minority layer type in the Hy3 hybrid, so the weight-read saving covers few layers and is cancelled by the norm->q8_1 fusion this path forgoes plus its gather/scatter copies. Primitive retained for a continuous-batching serve loop (batching across requests at higher m, no fused alternative to lose) |

| `MEMRA_PP_STAGES=<N>` / `MEMRA_PP_SPLITS=<c1,..,cN-1>` (`MEMRA_PP_SPLIT=<layer>` = the N=2 spelling) | pipeline-parallel N-stage runtime (M2 generalization of the M1 seam): decode_step runs the trunk as N stage subgraphs, EACH ON ITS OWN CUDA STREAM, handing the [n_embd] f32 residual through persistent double-buffered boundary slots at every cut (transport per boundary: dtod same-device / cudaMemcpyPeerAsync cross-device, evented TX/RX). Cuts default to the even split. Generic eager arm at any N; gemma4 eager at N=2. **Path coverage as of 2026-08-06** (this list used to say "batch/dc/graph/spec warn-once", which was wrong twice over — see the correction in `pp.rs`'s header): eager **and** batched (`MEMRA_BATCH_PP`) **and** spec verify (`MEMRA_SPEC_PP`) each take their own stage split; `decode_step_dc` and the graph capture that wraps it still run UNSPLIT, and they do NOT warn — `warn_unwired_once` has exactly two call sites and both are gemma4-specific. What protects dc/graph instead is failing closed: under an open door with a sharded cross-device placement they refuse via `pp::refuse_unsplit_if_remote` rather than silently peer-reading the remote stage's weights. Deferred-readback API (`decode_step_h_ppn_deferred` -> `PendingLogits`) keeps 2+ tokens in flight — scheduling only, math event-ordered per token | M1 (2026-08-01): BIT-IDENTICAL via pp2-gate, both models, every knob (`research/m1-pp2-20260801/`, `research/m1-inc2-20260801/`). M2 (2026-08-02, 8xH100): ppn-gate BIT-IDENTICAL at N=2/4/8 — SERIAL arm everywhere (the validated mode). PIPELINED (deferred) arm: same-device QUARANTINED (repro'd 35% co-located-stream race; the API refuses 2+ stage streams on one device, `MEMRA_PP_FORCE_SAME_DEV_PIPELINED=1` = soak override only) and cross-device ~1/70 flake (battery-5 dev01) — race root cause OPEN, deferred stays an experimental door until a x100+ clean soak; 1.87-1.88x decode at N=2/4 sharded is the prize (`research/m2-pp8-20260802/RESULTS.md`). Default OFF, zero behavior change unset |
| `MEMRA_PP_DEVICES=<d0>,..,<dN-1>` | ppN stage->device placement: stage s runs on device ds — the serving worker's primary Engine follows dN-1 (the HEAD stage's device: the sharded loader homes the lm head there and the spec round's draft chain + verify-logit consumers run on the primary, so any other pick peer-reads the head every draft token — the v0.72 112.5->17.5 spec+PP-2 regression; gate/bench binaries follow d0 by design), remote stages get their own Engine/context, KV for each layer range allocates on its stage's HBM (`Cache::new_ppn`), cross boundaries use cudaMemcpyPeerAsync on the publishing stream (M0: 2.8x NCCL at PP activation sizes; cuDeviceCanAccessPeer-guarded, peer-inaccessible = loud FAIL; peer + default-mempool access granted over every device pair in use). Ordinals are in CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES' process-local namespace; unset still defaults the worker to logical device 0. M2 increment 2: the LOADER uploads each stage's layer range (weights + decode mirrors) through that stage's engine — weights live where they run | default unset = all stages on the primary device. Cross-device gates on the 8x box: `MEMRA_PP_DEVICES=0,..,N-1 ppn-gate <model> <N>` |
| `MEMRA_PP_HOST_BOUNCE=1` | Broken-peer fallback for sharded cross-device PP: replace each cross-stage `cudaMemcpyPeerAsync` activation handoff with source-stream D2H into a geometry-sized page-locked host slot, an event, then destination-stream H2D. Two slots per boundary are allocated once from `PRIME_CHUNK_MAX_TOKENS * n_embd * sizeof(f32)` (64 MiB each for Step-3.7's `n_embd=4096`); decode and batch move only their exact payload prefix. Serving-time peer/pool grants are bypassed. When capability exists, boot diagnostics temporarily enable peer access and production-pool access to quote corrupt bytes, then revoke pool access and disable every probe-enabled peer pair before the host-staged path proceeds; teardown failure refuses startup. The same transport is now the automatic one-way runtime fallback after a native re-probe failure: the owner thread allocates the pinned slots, validates a real D2H/event/H2D row copy at every cross boundary, then atomically publishes host bounce before the next scheduler tick. Fail-closed safety doors reject `MEMRA_PP_SHARD=0`, unsplit remote-weight walks, and stream-mode spec's shared token/position UVA reads; serving also disables spec admission plus cross-device prefix/affinity snapshots. | Default OFF at startup: healthy PIX/NVLink hosts retain the existing peer path byte-for-byte. A runtime safety failure may promote it without restart; native P2P stays latched off for the process. Explicit startup opt-in remains available for a known-bad peer host; `research/hostbounce-20260810/`, runtime promotion `research/probehitch-20260812/`. |
| `MEMRA_PEER_PROBE=0` | Diagnostics-only escape hatch for PP-N's default-on byte-integrity gate. Normally every cross-device PP boundary is tested in both directions: a deterministic legacy-allocation 16 KiB `cudaMemcpyPeerAsync` preflight immediately after peer enable, then real stream-ordered `BoundarySlot` TX/RX copies at 1, 8, 16, and `PRIME_CHUNK_MAX_TOKENS` (4096) `[n_embd] f32` rows after pool grants and before weight upload. The final boot rung is one maximum production prime handoff (64 MiB at Step-3.7's `n_embd=4096`). Destination bytes are read back and compared exhaustively; logs include the largest clean payload. Native serving gives each runtime width its own staggered copy-count deadline (one width due per 8,192 native boundary TX copies, each width recurring every 32,768), so an overdue idle-only rung cannot block later cheap integrity checks. The maximum-production rung is idle-only before its first measurement; any other rung whose complete owner-thread pass exceeds the fixed 5 ms budget becomes idle-only on later cycles. Idle-only work runs only with no admitted, queued, or constraint-compilation work, after an empty command-channel check; late deadlines collapse rather than burst-catch up. A runnable cheap re-probe defers while a live stream-mode spec session still peer-reads token/position and verify state through UVA: forcing it is unsafe because a mismatch immediately revokes native peer access before host bounce is published, while host bounce covers boundary activations rather than those live spec pointers. Deferrals count once per 8,192-copy interval, not once per scheduler poll. `MEMRA_PEER_PROBE_DEFERRAL_BOUND` controls the consecutive-interval alarm and speculative soft-refusal point (default 4, one complete 32,768-copy rotation). At the bound, operator metric `peer_probe_integrity_degraded=true` and `SECURITY RED` publish, `/readyz.peer_probe_integrity` becomes `degraded`, and new speculative candidates take the existing plain path. Existing speculative sessions finish naturally; native transport is unchanged and no probe is forced under their live UVA state. Their retirement creates a safe boundary where a completed re-probe clears the streak and reopens speculative admission. Before the bound `/readyz` reports `deferred_<n>`; otherwise it reports `ok`. `peer_probe_deferred_total` remains cumulative. If recovery does not occur, drain speculative sessions or restart with `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC=0`, then require `peer_probe_runtime_reprobes` to increase and the advisory to clear; disabling the probe is never remediation. A runtime mismatch or probe error at a safe boundary is SECURITY RED and one-way latches native P2P off. New admissions run plain after that transport degradation. The worker continues on host bounce only after pinned staging passes its real copy/readback validation; it panics only if that staging cannot be armed or validated. `MEMRA_PEER_PROBE=0` REFUSES a sharded cross-device native placement when `MEMRA_PP_HOST_BOUNCE!=1`; with explicit host bounce it logs SECURITY RED and increments operator metric `peer_probe_bypassed`. Probe-off remains harmless for single-device or non-sharded diagnostic placements. Operator metrics expose native boundary copies, completed runtime probes, failures, deferrals, current overdue-integrity state, and `peer_probe_degraded_to_host_bounce`. | Default unset/on. Never disable in serving; use only to isolate probe-specific bring-up failures, with `MEMRA_PP_HOST_BOUNCE=1` on sharded cross-device placement. Design and vendor basis: `research/cx-peerprobe-20260811/DESIGN.md`; large-rung extension and costs: `research/sec9-20260812/`; runtime scheduling/failover: `research/probehitch-20260812/`; bounded deferral: `research/probeobs-20260812/`; bounded self-recovery: `research/probeready-20260812/`. |
| `MEMRA_PEER_PROBE_DEFERRAL_BOUND=<n>` | Consecutive deferred runtime-probe copy intervals allowed before `/readyz.peer_probe_integrity` becomes `degraded` and new speculative candidates are served plain until a completed probe clears the streak. This changes admission only: existing speculative sessions and all plain admissions continue, and it never authorizes a probe under live UVA state. Read once by the worker at startup. | Default `4`; minimum `1` (`0` clamps to `1`); invalid values use `4`. Four is the probeobs one-rotation safety bound; raise only with an explicit integrity-coverage policy. |
| `MEMRA_PP_SHARD=0` | rollback to the M1 bring-up weight placement: everything uploads through the primary engine; remote stages peer-read weights over NVLink/PCIe | rollback seam only; sharded placement is the default whenever `MEMRA_PP_DEVICES` is set (gated bit-identical both ways at N=2/4/8) |
| `MEMRA_PP_OVERLAP` | ppN double-buffered boundary slot ALTERNATION: TX(t+1)'s write-after-read guard waits only the previous use of ITS slot, so the deferred loop issues token t+1 stage 0 while token t's later stages run. Scheduling structure only — every token's math is event-ordered. Unset FOLLOWS the dual-PP mode since the 2026-08-11 default flip: unset resolves ON under the naked default (Auto), OFF under `MEMRA_DUAL_PP=0` (so one flag restores the exact pre-flip serial path) and OFF under the explicit `MEMRA_DUAL_PP=1` request (so the binding single-slot refusal stays reachable). Explicit `1`/`0` always wins. | default follows dual-PP mode (ON naked); gated bit-identical (ppn-gate arm 2, decode-batch-gate dual matrix, box1 re-gate soak) |
| `MEMRA_DUAL_PP` | **default ON (Auto) since 2026-08-11 (owner flip)** — dual-active plain-decode for PP-2. Three states: unset = Auto (dual runs exactly where the box1 re-gate validated it — PP-2 fence, double-slot boundary, peer transport, B>=2 — and silently degrades to the serial PP-N walker everywhere else, incl. naked PP-3 and `MEMRA_PP_HOST_BOUNCE=1`); `0` = serial rollback seam (also resolves unset overlap OFF, restoring the exact pre-flip naked path); `1` = the explicit pre-flip request, which ROUTES every B>=2 PP-2 call into the dual body so an ineligible placement refuses loudly with the binding quoted reason (single-slot, host-bounce) before any token or cache advance. The worker treats the model's exact batch cap as a per-wave cap and schedules up to two caps in one tick with a balanced midpoint (A=`ceil(c/2)`, B=`floor(c/2)`); c=1 remains the honest serial path. The dual body prepares both slots up front and calls only the always-alternating `prepare_overlap_slots` / `tx_pipelined` primitives. `/metrics.dual_pp` (operator-only): completed slot pairs, per-slot uses, rejected same-slot collisions, timing, topology. `MEMRA_DUAL_PP_TIMING=1` adds CUDA-event spans (never fails decode; excluded from scored runs). | FLIPPED to default after the box1 PRO-pair re-gate (research/dualpp2-20260811/RESULTS-regate.md): bit-identity B=1..5, servestress no-thrash (admission delta serial==dual 0/0/0), 10-boot soak 929/929 golden per arm + 0 slot collisions across 9123 pairs; perf floor receipts research/dualpp1-20260811/RESULTS.md (c8 +20.753% min, rising to c10 +30.5%). Safety regressions: pp.rs flip_* tests, worker.rs flip_naked_default test, kernel-check `dual-pp-default-flip` manifest cell. |
| `MEMRA_PP_STREAMS=0` | ppN rollback seam to the increment-1 same-stream boundary (two plain dtod copies per cut, no per-stage streams/events/devices; generalized to N) | rollback only; the per-stage stream choreography is the default when the door is open |
| `MEMRA_BATCH_PP=0` | **rollback seam, DEFAULT ON** (pp2-batch 2026-08-06): with the ppN door open the BATCHED decode step takes its own stage split (`decode_step_batch_ppn`) exactly as the eager step does. `=0` sends the batched path back through the unsplit body, where a sharded cross-device placement is then caught by `refuse_unsplit_if_remote` (the 28x peer-read regime) rather than running silently. Read per step and never memoized, so the bit-identity gate can A/B split-vs-unsplit IN ONE PROCESS against the same loaded weights | Exactness: `decode-batch-gate --mode pp` 0 differing bits on all 7 configs (dev01/dev10/singledev/split5/N=4/q27/wide). Cost: 0.5-1.5% at B=4/8/16, transport 0.986-0.997x of the seam. `research/pp2-batch-20260806/` |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PP=0` | **rollback seam, DEFAULT ON** (pp2-spec 2026-08-06): with the ppN door open the spec VERIFY forward (`decode_step_t_core_ppn`, T=K+1) takes its own stage split. `=0` sends verify back through the unsplit trunk walk, caught by the same guard. Read per verify call, never memoized, for the same in-process A/B reason. **This is exactness plumbing, not the serving engagement policy** — `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE` decides whether PP-2 admits spec, and defaults it off on throughput evidence. | Exactness: `decode-batch-gate --mode ppspec` 7/7 arms ALL GREEN. The #87 reverse-publication fix closed the old fatal concurrency placement (212/212 requests at c=2..8); v0.72 made placement orders throughput-symmetric. Spec remains callable through `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE=0`, but loses every current PP-2 c=1/2/4 policy cell. `research/pp2-spec-20260806/`, `research/pp2spec-crash-20260807/`, `research/specplace-20260808/` |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_PP=0` | **rollback seam for the PRIME (chunked prefill) stage split** (lane/pp-leverb 2026-08-08): with the ppN door open the chunked prime takes its own per-stage range walk, like the eager/batched/verify steps. `=0` sends prime back through the unsplit whole-trunk walk. Unlike the other four paths prime keeps **NO** `refuse_unsplit_if_remote`: its unsplit walk over a sharded placement is the measured **22% amortized peer-read tax** (m=4096 amortizes the weight reads — not the decode 28x cliff), and it is the `prime-split-gate`'s REFERENCE arm, so it must stay callable. Read per call, never memoized (the gate A/Bs both arms in one process) | Gate: `prime-split-gate` (`ppsplit`/`ppsplitc` fast-gate rows) — split-vs-unsplit bit-identity (last-row logits, h_seed, [T,n_embd] hiddens, teacher-forced decode steps over the primed KV) PLUS the `pp::PRIME_SPLIT_CHUNKS` liveness counter. Registered RED 2026-08-08, **GREEN at the walker commit** (`prime_chunk_ppn`): SPLIT BIT-IDENTICAL + LIVE at T=4883, canary teeth proven. Perf: pp4096 **141.4 → 266.1 tok/s (1.88x)**, N=5 interleaved. `research/pp-leverb-20260807/` |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_PIPE=0` | rollback seam for PP-2 prime **chunk pipelining** (lane/cx-pipeline-prime 2026-08-08). Unset keeps the pipelined schedule when the prime stage split has at least two chunks; `=0` retains Lever B's serial chunk walk over the same effective chunk boundaries, including the naked PP-2 auto geometry above. Scheduling only: the serial split remains the bit-exact arithmetic oracle | Gate extends `ppsplit` with serial-split vs pipelined replay plus concurrent-active-walker `pp::PRIME_PIPE_OVERLAPS` liveness; receipts `research/pipeprime-20260808/` |
| `MEMRA_PP_ALLOW_UNSPLIT_BATCH=1` | MEASUREMENT DOOR: re-admits **all four unsplit decode paths** — `decode_step_batch`, `decode_step_dc`, the graph capture that wraps dc, and `decode_step_t*` spec verify (one door, one shared guard `pp::refuse_unsplit_if_remote`, because it is one measurement question) — under an open ppN door with a sharded cross-device placement. Since 2026-08-06 that combination **refuses** (fails closed), because the batched trunk has no stage split — it walks all layers on the primary stream and peer-reads every remote stage's weights each step. Exactness is unaffected (peer reads return identical bytes and all three `decode-batch-gate` gates PASS), so the previous behavior was a silent perf cliff with a green battery: measured **7.4 vs 208.9 tok/s at B=1 (28x), 47.4 vs 657.0 at B=8 (13.9x)** on 2x RTX PRO 6000 over Gen5 x16 P2P. Same fail-closed precedent as the deferred-pipelined arm's same-device refusal | pp2-hardening 2026-08-06, receipts `research/pp2-hardening-20260806/` (RESULTS.jsonl, `logs/batchcost/`). Non-measurement escapes: `MEMRA_PP_SHARD=0` (weights all home — restores full speed, forfeits the capacity PP-2 exists for) or close the pp door for batched serving. **Superseded in part the same day**: "wiring a real stage split is the open weeks-class item" was true when this door landed, and is no longer — `MEMRA_BATCH_PP` (batched) and `MEMRA_SPEC_PP` (verify) both ship split and default ON, so the door now only covers `decode_step_dc`, its graph wrapper, and explicit `*_PP=0` rollbacks. Gate: 20 arms across 5 binaries, 0 unexpected results — incl. `devices=0,0` NOT refusing (over-broad-predicate check) and `run-spec` acceptance rates identical with the override on vs the door shut (proving it is a door, not a behavior change) |
| `MEMRA_EAGLE` / `MEMRA_EAGLE_ALIGN=0` | EAGLE draft lane (run-eagle bin; ALIGN=0 = un-shifted MTP-style pairing A/B) | experimental lane, not on the daily path |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_DRAFT_GRAPH=1` | gemma draft chain replays as ONE captured CUDA graph (keyed kr/rung/window-regime; pos slots + g_seed are eager-filled graph inputs) | steady-state perf ~0 (launch tax hidden at 96.7% busy) — exists as the BURST enabler; capture-retain allocator mode ships with it (2026-07-12) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_SPEC_BURST=<M>` (+`_DRAFT_GRAPH=1`) | pre-issue M full spec rounds with ONE host sync per burst (verify-stream + device accept/seed/rollback/ring on the round_stream generics) | EXACT (stream 128/128 short+depth, 26B+31B) but PERF-NEGATIVE single-stream — no draft/verify overlap materializes on one stream and fixed-K costs the adaptive policy (26B short 304 vs 379; 31B 74 vs 88). Default 0. Stage 2 = second-stream speculative draft(N+1) under verify(N) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_ROUND_GRAPH=1` | the WHOLE gemma spec round (draft chain + stream verify + device accept/seed/rollback/commit + `spec_adapt_k` device adaptive depth) captured once per regime, ONE graph launch per round | EXACT (26B 64/64 short + 128/128 depth, 31B 64/64) with BETTER round dynamics (tok/round 5.33 vs 4.74) but PERF-NEGATIVE at fixed shapes (−10/−11% interleaved, 2026-07-13) — the replay pays fixed k_cap drafts + fixed-width verify, not launch gaps (the enqueue-ahead law, 4th confirmation). Door kept for the exec-update-width phase 2; 31B pair pending a valid window |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_BURST_GRAPH=1` | capture the gemma DRAFT CHAIN as one graph per (k_cap, dc bucket, window regime) inside the spec burst loop (`gemma_spec.rs`) | experimental door beside `MEMRA_GEMMA_SPEC_BURST` — same lane, same verdict class; default off |
| `MEMRA_G4PLAIN_EAGER=1` | gemma4 PLAIN-serving graph loop bisect: run the step body eagerly every step (no capture/replay) — splits "body semantics wrong" from "replay mechanics wrong" (the round-graph law; `gemma_spec.rs`) | bisect seam, not a config |
| `MEMRA_ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PRETOKENIZER=1` | downgrade the unknown-pre-tokenizer **hard load error** to a loud per-load WARN, then run the `qwen35` split anyway (`memra-tokenizer/src/lib.rs`; supported: `qwen35`, `qwen2`, `deepseek-v3`, `gemma4` — gemma-4's absent `pre` key is allowlisted explicitly, on a tensor-census audit, not by falling through) | **Produces WRONG TOKEN IDS by construction.** Before 2026-08-19 this was the DEFAULT behaviour and it was silent: one `eprintln!` per process, then a fall-through to the qwen35 split — the model loaded, generated fluent text, and every id was wrong. Same fluent-and-invisible class as the GGUF chat-template mint trap. Wrong ids poison goldens, parity fixtures, acceptance counts and every quality number downstream. Set it ONLY for deliberate bring-up experimentation on an unported family, and never for a run whose numbers will be quoted (lane/engine-hardening-20260819) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_FA_ONE_PROGRAM=1` | route dense-gemma SWA prefill attention through the WINDOWED arm at EVERY prompt length instead of switching program at `t > sliding_window` (`hybrid_forward.rs gemma_fa_one_program()`) | **Names a live prefix-restore exactness violation, and does not fix it by default.** Below the switch the SWA layers attend unwindowed on f32 operands, above it windowed on bf16, so rows `[0..932)` of a 932-row prime and of a 1048-row prime differ in **59 of 60 layers** — the published prefix KV depends on total prompt length. `=1` collapses it to one program so it stops depending on length. **Default OFF** because collapsing changes the bytes short prompts get today; the flip is an owner call (lane/gemma-restore-exactness-20260819) |

### Diagnostics for the gemma prefix-restore arc (lane/gemma-restore-exactness-20260819)

| flag | what it does |
|---|---|
| `MEMRA_KVPROBE_AT=<n[,n…]>` | digest the FIRST `n` rows of a LIVE cache, with `n <= cache.pos` allowed (`worker.rs kvprobe_ats()`). `prefix_cache_state_digest` refuses `n != pos`, which makes it unable to answer the question the arc turns on: are the KV bytes a COLD 1048-row prime writes for rows `[0..932)` the same bytes a 932-row prime wrote — and therefore published, and therefore restored? Unset = the probe costs nothing |
| `MEMRA_KVPROBE_LAYERS=1` | with `..._AT` set, also emit the per-layer digests instead of the whole-state digest alone (`worker.rs kvprobe_layers()`) — how the 59-of-60-layer divergence above was localized |

| flag | door | status |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_WGMMA=1` | hand-rolled wgmma GEMM v0 (unpipelined 64x64 tile, `wait_group<0>` every 32-K step) instead of MMQ | ~3x SLOWER per launch at m=512 model shapes (the "688us ref" was a pp2048-shape figure); default stays MMQ until a pipelined version beats it N=5. Correctness pinned regardless — kernel-check's wgmma case is cfg-gated, not env-gated (`lib.rs wgmma_gemm_enabled()`) |
| `MEMRA_FA_P1=1` | non-windowed prefill FA P1 arm at hd256: the engine-study body (FA2 schedule + boundary split + swizzle), bf16 pre-convert | opt-in until the qwen battery (`lib.rs fa_prefill`) |
| `MEMRA_FA_PP_W2=1` | 2-warp/32-row CTA tile for the f32 pp prefill FA — doubles grid.x, bit-identical per-row math, pure coverage trade for the 6.25%-occupancy starvation | ncu probe 2026-07-26; also disables the batched varlen FA arm while set (`lib.rs`, `hybrid_forward.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_FA_SP16=1` | gemma FA-decode split ladder alternative: t_kv-keyed {16, 64, 128} instead of the mixed default | measured on 188-SM rigs only (rig-divergence law — the 5090 ladder keeps its validated default); probe seam (`lib.rs fa_split_keys`) |
| `MEMRA_Q80_CA=1` | q8_0 `rpca` cp.async-staged weight-ring matvec (needs in_f % 1024 == 0) | MEASURED NEGATIVE on H100 2026-07-26 (181.8 vs plain rp 185.5 — the smem round-trip exceeds the latency it hides for 8-bit direct-dp4a); opt-in for the corpus |

| `MEMRA_Q40_MR=1\|2` | q4_0 m=1 / fused-t=1 rows-per-thread override | default mr1 at the plain m=1 site; the fused-t=1 site defaults per-model (`FUSED_MR1_DEFAULT`: dense gemma mr1, MoE mr2) — env wins both (`lib.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_Q40_BV=base\|r2\|ms\|sm\|la` | force ONE q4_0 batched-verify variant (the q4_0 twin of the documented `MEMRA_MMVQ_BV`/`MEMRA_KQ_BV`) | `ms`/`sm`/`la` are force-only measurement arms — ALL FLAT/NEGATIVE 2026-07-13, never auto (`lib.rs`) |

Non-class archs and t>16 take rowwise anyway; ROWWISE=1 stays the exactness oracle/rollback seam (`spec.rs`) |

### The DFlash draft-head block (`memra-engine/src/dflash.rs`; gemma-gate cell AND the shared drafter behind the `MEMRA_DSPARK_SPEC` serve route)

DFlash is the block-diffusion drafter bring-up (DFLASH-BRINGUP-PLAN.md, 2026-07-13): a 5-layer
qwen3-class mini-transformer drafting a 16-token block in ONE non-causal forward, conditioned on
the target's tapped hidden states. The gemma-gate cell is armed by **`MEMRA_SPEC_DFLASH=<dflash dir>`**
— which REFUSES when combined with `MEMRA_DRAFT` or `MEMRA_GEN_CORPUS`/`_OUT`
(different drafters / different runs; the wrong branch would win silently — 2026-08-16 incident).
The same `dflash.rs` `DflashDraft` is ALSO what the serving-side dspark route loads
(`MEMRA_DSPARK_SPEC=1`, worker.rs — one `DflashDraft` per non-gemma model; catalogued in §5),
so this file does reach HTTP traffic when that door is armed.

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_PREC` | `q4` | drafter weight precision: `q4` = all q4_0 (DEFAULT since the head-trim lane 2026-08-25, owner-ratified — PRO 6000 gate x3 157.9 vs q8 152.4 spec tok/s at unchanged acceptance 0.662/0.656, ALL EXACT; 5090 masked-head ABBA 83.85 -> 87.10 E2E tok/s); `q8` = all q8_0 (1.6 GB, the pre-flip default = rollback seam); `mixed` = bf16 attn+fc (the ctx-conditioning path) + q8_0 ffn (~2.2 GB); `bf16` = all bf16 (parity runs, no target). Boot prints `[dspark] precision=<resolved> (MEMRA_DFLASH_PREC set|unset)`; unknown values, including the measured-defective `q5`, refuse rather than silently falling back to q8. |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_VERIFY_T` | 8 (clamp 2..block) | verify width: the drafter always drafts the full block (its trained mask pattern) but only the first vt rows go through target verify — t=16 rides the untuned b16 tier (~65 ms/verify) while b8 rides the tuned r2 tier, and with ~2.7 committed/round the deep positions almost never survive. Exactness unaffected (verify still decides every committed token) |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_ADAPT` | on | adaptive verify width (the MTP accepted+1 recipe): next round verifies one past this round's accepted run, clamp [3, cap]; `0` = fixed width. On a head-carrying dspark checkpoint the resolved default policy is `confidence-slot` (below); `MEMRA_DFLASH_ADAPT=0` is read as an explicit fixed-window request and keeps the ladder-family arm |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_HARVEST` | checkpoint census | draft-row harvest convention for DFlash-family block drafters: `dspark` = shifted all-rows (anchor row's output = draft 1), `dflash` = historical mask-fill rows 1..b-1. Resolution is FAMILY-keyed first, then strategy-keyed (v0.100 train): a DFlash2 checkpoint is mask-fill BY CONSTRUCTION (reference `dflash_generate` harvests rows `1-verify_size:`) — it always harvests `dflash`, and `dspark` on a DFlash2 checkpoint REFUSES loudly (census-keyed, never env-keyed). Every other checkpoint: UNSET resolves from the CHECKPOINT's own training-strategy census (`architectures` naming a DSpark class / `dflash_config.projector_type == "dspark"`) — owner-ratified flip 2026-08-20 after B1 (mismatched convention = the accept 2.9→1.43 collapse, DSPARK-POSTMORTEM-20260820). Unknown values refuse; the resolved harvest is printed at drafter load (`[dspark] harvest=… (checkpoint census dflash2=… strategy_dspark=…)`) |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_VT` | `confidence-slot` (head census) | verify-window policy: `confidence-slot` = longest draft prefix whose EVERY slot clears tau on the accept-rate head's own sigmoid (owner directive "take only high confidence offers"); `confidence` = cumprod-survival threshold; `ladder` = the reactive accepted+1 ladder. UNSET defaults to `confidence-slot` when the checkpoint carries the accept-rate head (owner-ratified H4 flip 2026-08-20: 94–98% of fixed-8 accept at wall ≥ ladder, exactness 11/11) and to `ladder` when it does not (nothing to schedule with) or when `MEMRA_DFLASH_ADAPT=0`. Resolved policy printed at drafter load (`[dspark] verify-window=…`) |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_VT_TAU` | 0.5 | confidence threshold in (0,1) for both confidence window arms, including the resolved default (H4 cell-3 tau ladder: τ=.5 is the knee for the slot arm; τ=.35 is the wall-max point for the survival arm). Bad values refuse |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_BATCH_SMOKE=N` | off | dsv4 CPU dspark gate (`dsv4_dspark_gate` bin) only: truncate the batched-vs-sequential comparison to N new tokens — a fast-machinery smoke for the gate itself, never a serving knob. Arrived with the it5 merge; unmasked when the v0.98 scope-note carve-out narrowed to `MEMRA_DSV4_*` (main CI's census caught it — the narrowing doing exactly its job) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_SPEC_DEPTH=N` | unbounded | dsv4 serve door (lane/dsv4-flash-revival-20260822): cap the per-round draft depth on the spec route — exactly the bench drivers' depth policy, exposed at the serving seam. Identity-gated: every depth reproduces the plain route token-for-token (rung-1/rung-3 receipts in darklanes) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_VT=off\|fixed@K\|slot@TAU` | off | dsv4 serve door: the confidence-window verify policy (dspark vtconf H4). `off` = full-depth ladder verify; `fixed@K` = fixed window; `slot@TAU` = per-slot confidence window at tau (owner-directive per-slot arm; the head emits MARGINAL accept probs so per-slot is the correct reading). Default stays `off` until the owner ratifies the flip — recommended `slot@0.5` per the banked H4 verdict |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_VT_TAU=T` / `MEMRA_DSV4_VT_FLOOR=F` | 0.5 / 0 | dsv4 serve door: tau and window floor for the `slot` policy when `MEMRA_DSV4_VT` names it without inline values |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_DRAFTER=dspark` | unset (trunk-only, plain routes) | dsv4 serve door: load the checkpoint's own DSpark drafter (~10.7 GiB resident on the second card) and arm the spec routes; unset = drafter absent, plain decode only (route keying reads drafter residency) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_DEVICES=a,b` | `0,1` | dsv4 serve door: which two CUDA devices carry the PP-2 stack (`Dsv4Gpu` is two-card by construction); unparseable = boot refusal |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_DEFER_READBACK` | on | engine-bundle slice 2: the dspark verify runs with DEVICE tokens and no blocking chain readback — one merged DtoH sync per round instead of two (draft-chain read + verify argmax read). `0` = the two-sync host-token arm (rollback seam; byte identity between the arms is pinned by the E2E gate, and the device-token embed rides the same `embed_gather_u32_t` rows). Refuses under a PP stage split (no stage-split arm) |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_VERIFY_GRAPH` | ROUTE-SPLIT: serve route ON, bin arm off | engine-bundle slice 3 + graphs-serve lane: replay per-(segment, vt) CUDA graphs for the dspark verify's consecutive LINEAR-layer runs, plus the full-verify (vt, rung, hi) graph when a round rides one seqs rung (captured from the same `qwen35_tparallel_linear_layer`/fa-layer bodies the eager arm runs — no second copy of the math; pointer tables refreshed per verify). SERVE ROUTE DEFAULT ON since v0.103 (owner-ratified 2026-08-22 on the §10 serve-lifetime battery: crossover K=36–43 requests, steady −0.357 ms/round, session wall −1.55..−1.65%, byte-exact 240/240 ×3 interleaved boot pairs; pool = model-owned, shared across sessions, measured 8,852 MiB at storm-complete on the q38 export) — `MEMRA_DSPARK_VERIFY_GRAPH=0` is the kill-switch (eager walk, byte-identical), `MEMRA_DSPARK_VG_MAX=0` the finer freeze valve. COMPOSITION CONSTRAINT (measured on the v0.103 train battery): the door rides the slice-2 deferred path ONLY, and the confidence-slot vt policy (the v0.101 default on accept-rate-head checkpoints under adaptive) keeps the legacy readback order — so on a head-carrying drafter at the adaptive route default the pool is structurally inert (0 captures, exactness unchanged); ladder/fixed arms (`MEMRA_DFLASH_ADAPT=0`, `MEMRA_DFLASH_VERIFY_T=k`) and head-less drafters (DFlash2) engage it. BIN ARM stays opt-in (`=1`) by its own measured disposition: at gate scale the mean is FLAT (109.2 vs 109.4 — the capture toll is never repaid over a 256-token run). dspark-only; MTP untouched; refuses to co-arm with the ROUND-STREAM verify. ADMISSION ACCOUNTING (2026-08-23, lane/hermes-perf-fixes): the serve gate charges the pool's projected remaining growth (`dspark_vg_debt_projection`: remaining capture slots x the MARGINAL bytes a new key adds to the device graph-mem pool, two-point measured) on top of the transient reserve for dspark-armed models, so sessions admitted while the pool is cold no longer overcommit the VRAM it will hold; `MEMRA_DSPARK_VG_MAX` doubles as the reserve's tightening valve (=0 zeroes it with the freeze). MARGINAL, not mean, by on-box measurement: with the q38 pool's reservation flat at ~33.6 MiB across captures 1..3 a mean-based reading extrapolated the pool's ONE-TIME shared allocation and charged 8,556/4,261/2,830 MB of phantom reserve — an over-reserve that can refuse admissions that would have fit. Until growth is measurable (single observation) the charge is bounded to one more pool's worth |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_MARKOV` | on | greedy on-device Markov semi-AR chain over the block (logits_k += W2(W1[prev realized token])) when the drafter carries the markov tensors; `0` = plain parallel block |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_EMB_SCALE` | off (raw) | `1` = feed the drafter sqrt(n_embd)-scaled embed rows — whether z-lab's gemma4 training fed scaled or raw is not visible from the reference; acceptance arbitrates |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_FA` | off (sdpa_naive) | set = block attention through `fa_prefill` instead of the naive SDPA (the fa arm returns behind this seam once its kernel is fixed + parity-gated) |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_NOROPE` | off | set = skip rope on the draft q (bring-up bisect arm) |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_DUMP=<dir>` | off | stage dumps (ctx_features, per-layer q/k, layer outputs; f32 LE) for the oracle parity bisect |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_DEBUG=1` | off | per-round debug prints (first-round block state etc.) |

## 6. Research platform (MTP-heal, dual-shape)

memra's second shape is a research platform. The first protocol (MTP-heal) measures MTP draft-head
acceptance at FULL PRECISION as the ceiling, then on the NVFP4 daily GGUF — the delta is the quant
hit on drafting. Recorded in the dual-shape lane notes, archived 2026-08-17.

| flag | what it does |
|---|---|
| `MEMRA_FULL_PREC=1` | FULL-PRECISION LOADER MODE (default OFF). Bypasses the standing loader law (large BF16/F8 → Q8_0/NVFP4 re-encode, the "Float-poison" tripwire — the very re-encodes this mode must NOT do). Everything loads Float; large 2D bf16 matmul weights stay **bf16-resident** (`GpuTensor::FloatBf16`) with dequant-on-use, so the 9B (~18GB bf16) + f32 activations fit 24GB instead of blowing to ~38GB as an all-f32 materialization. Compute rides the Stage-A f32 oracle path end to end (`MEMRA_FAST=0`-class). **SLOW IS FINE** — this mode is for the exactness ceiling, not speed. Also forces the EAGER spec draft (CUDA-graph capture can't enclose cuBLASLt f32 GEMV or the dequant alloc) and disables `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` (the ceiling wants the model's natural full head; the trim gather is Quant-only anyway). The Float-poison tripwire warnings are correct here and are suppressed. |

Per-slot acceptance for this protocol comes from the existing **`MEMRA_SPEC_STATS=1`** (§4) — its
`per_slot=[...]` line is the deep-K decay profile; no separate flag was needed.

**Acceptance battery harness** (`tools/`, no GPU-free CI — runs on the rig):
- `acceptance_battery.sh <model> <out.jsonl>` — fixed prompt set (p1/p2/p3) + the 8-turn agent loop, N≥3, one JSONL row per (prompt,K,run). `FULL_PREC=1` for the bf16 ceiling arm, plain for the NVFP4 arm.
- `agent_loop_acceptance.sh <model> <out.jsonl> <arm>` — the 8-turn accumulative agent-loop protocol (recreated in-repo; feeds each turn's output forward).
- `acceptance_parse.py` — one run-spec invocation (merged stdout+stderr, `MEMRA_SPEC_K=<k>` + `MEMRA_SPEC_STATS=1`) → one JSONL row.
- `acceptance_delta.py <bf16.jsonl> <nvfp4.jsonl>` — the deliverable delta table: per-(prompt,K) median acceptance, ceiling vs quant, and the hit.

```
# bf16 ceiling:
FULL_PREC=1 tools/acceptance_battery.sh /data/ai-ml/hf-models/qwen35-9b-hf out-bf16.jsonl
# NVFP4 hit:
tools/acceptance_battery.sh /data/ai-ml/hf-models/qwen35-9b-nvfp4-gguf/Qwen3.5-9B-NVFP4-MTP-GGUF.gguf out-nvfp4.jsonl
# delta:
tools/acceptance_delta.py out-bf16.jsonl out-nvfp4.jsonl --json summary.json
```
