## 7. The H100 / sm_90a lane (merged from `backend/sm90a-boot` rounds 26-36; unified-tree rounds 37-49)

On an H100 the arch auto-detects (`MEMRA_CUDA_ARCH=90a` forces it). Every promoted
config below followed the repo law:
harness proof -> engine seam -> full battery (kernel-check pins, greedy/stream identity,
decode/prime/graph gates, interleaved x5 A/B) -> default-ON with a `=0` revert. The
evidence ledger is [ARCHITECTURE-H100.md](../ARCHITECTURE-H100.md); the one-command
battery is `tools/validate-h100.sh <model.gguf> [--quick]`.

### Promoted defaults (Hopper; `=0` reverts each)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_GDN_MMA` | ON (90a + 120a) | GDN K4/K5 chunk pair on mma tensor cores (bf16 W/k mirrors); kernel-check pins both configs. sm_120a naked default since lane/moeprime-nvfp4-direct (2026-08-21): bf16 HMMA is sm_80-class PTX; measured +5-8% on a RTX PRO 6000 (ornith15 pp14715) and +1-2% on the local 5090 (q38-27b), interleaved both orders. One const default (`gdn_mma_default_on`) feeds all three read sites — a partial flip arms the mma pre-work while the scan runs scalar (measured net loss) |
| `MEMRA_GDN_WGMMA` | ON (90a only) | K4+K5 FUSED wgmma kernel (`gdn_k45_wgmma`) + K2 on wgmma; Y/Ssnap tensors never materialized; qb16/Pb16 mirrors; nested inside the mma config. Official prefill +2.56%, batched prime +3.8%. HARD-gated to Hopper builds (2026-08-21): the wgmma asm bodies exist only at `__CUDA_ARCH__==900` (`MEMRA_K45_REAL`) — on other arches the kernel compiles empty, so the env force is refused rather than silently producing garbage |
| `MEMRA_FA3` | ON (90a) | FA3 full-attn prefill: TMA swizzled Q/K/V ring + wgmma, 4.8x the mma kernel (v11) |
| `MEMRA_L2_V2` | ON | l2-norm v2 with in-epilogue bf16 mirror folds (kb16 + qb16 — the standalone mirror passes are gone) |
| `MEMRA_GDN_DB` | ON | de-broadcast q/k at num_k heads (`hk` plumbed through the chunked chain) |
| `MEMRA_EMBED_DEV` | ON | device-side embed gather (host dequant + htod per token gone; +12.4% official lane) |
| `MEMRA_GEN_GRAPH` | ON at budget >= 48 (>=180 SMs) / >= 256 (everything else) | hybrid graph door in `generate_with`: capture/replay decode over the batched-prime cache. Official decode 190.3 -> 220.7 tok/s (+16%); bit-identical (graph-decode-gate, 256 steps x 16 fa buckets). `1` forces at any budget. KEY 256 -> 48 ON >=180-SM RIGS ONLY (q27 deep dive 2026-08-05): the 256 key was the E4B amortization rule, never a measured crossover — every <=128-token generation (incl. the board shape) ran eager on 188-SM silicon where the crossover is actually ~48 (two-model sweep, N=3 interleaved: q8 +3.80% / nvfp4 +7.72% at n=128, receipts `research/q27-deepdive-20260805/`). SM-GATED at 180 because the 48 key does NOT transfer to the 82-SM laptop 5090 (same protocol: -1.61% at n=128, negative through n=512, `local5090/` receipts) and the 132-SM H100 / 170-SM desktop 5090 are unmeasured at sub-256 budgets — those rigs keep the 256 key their board rows were measured with; widening below 180 needs an on-box crossover sweep |
| `MEMRA_CONV_FUSE` | ON | carried-ring conv + SiLU + GDN repack in one pass (bit-identical class) |
| `MEMRA_GDN_VL` | ON | varlen batched cores: ONE launch per stage for all B sequences (bit-gateable vs per-seq) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_F16G` | ON (mode 2 BOTH arches, silu class — 90a flipped 2026-08-02 at 152.6% of cublas; sm_120a sk + direct tiles) | grouped f16 expert prefill lane (round 49 promotion): q35 board prime 5490 -> 8380 (+53%) at 41/41 dequant coverage — the last board loss flipped. Round 50: the gemma-MoE (gelu) site defaults OFF per model (g26 board-2048 prefill -8.3% under the round-49 default; `=1`/`=2` still opens it). sm_120a MODE-2 default (2026-08-02, lane/f16g-default-rearb): with the IQ4_XS/IQ3_S direct loaders the sk visitor beats the int8-MMA MMQ tiles on the IQ banks too — naked q35 board-2048 4023 -> 5356 (+33.1%), KAT pp512 2029 -> 2988 (+47.2%) / pp2048 2973 -> 3886 (+30.7%), decode flat (t>=16 floor), o35b + g26 dispatch-unchanged (sha-identical), x5/x3 interleaved (receipts `research/f16g-default-rearb-20260802/`). AUTO-KQUANT (mode 3, lane/q4k-expert-prefill — sk only where MMQ rejects the layer; Ornith-35B Q4_K board-2048 1098 -> 3454, 3.14x) stays reachable as `=3`, the rollback seam. Modes + full history in §5; `=0` reverts |
| `MEMRA_PP_F16` | ON (90a), per-model; UNSET elsewhere = capacity-keyed auto | f16 prefill mirrors on the cuBLASLt lane (dequant-once at load, decode untouched — m>=16 arm only). With the env unset, `pp_f16_capacity_ok` (f16_ffi.rs, zoo-fusion arc 2026-08-17) admits mirrors iff free VRAM covers the admissible f16 mass + 8 GiB serving headroom (measured c8 agg +37%, ttft −70% on the 31B downQ6K trunk; 24GB rigs refuse by construction); an explicit env keeps absolute priority. Coverage classes: Q8_0 (2026-07-26, pp512 +80%), Q6_K (round 47, q27 pp2048 +64%), Q4_K + Q5_K (round 49, q27 pp2048 +54% at default budget). PER-MODEL argmax-gate arbitration (round 45): the qwen Q8_0 dense class defaults OFF (the f16-vs-int8 numeric gap flips a real-prompt top-tie deterministically — gate-violating defaults don't ship); gemma + the MoE hybrids hold MATCH and keep mirrors. `=1` forces (diagnostic), `=0` kills everywhere |
| `MEMRA_PP_F16_BUDGET_MB` | 32768 | VRAM cap for the f16 mirrors, layer-order prefix; admission passes ordered Q6_K -> Q4_K -> Q5_K so full Q6_K coverage (the ~10x dequant-GEMM replacement) is the floor. Machine-specific config: q27 serving at 43008 measured pp2048 6564 (+33% over default; +Q5_K full stack 8063). On 80GB the f16-mirror budget and the KQRP decode mirrors compete for the same ~15GB — per-box serving configs choose (round 49b) |

### Serving (memra-server)

One-class correctness default (2026-08-13): ordinary serving uses the generic batched decode
program at B=1 as well as B>=2. The eager B=1 fusion program and GraphSession replay are each
solo-only; changing co-residence used to switch a live request between their FP composition and
the batched composition. That changed real token ids in Step35 and Q35-MoE, then reproduced the
Q27 restored-hit failure exactly: controlled peer arrival selected the historical 11-token EOS
hash at 50 and 225 ms, while B1FAST=0 held restored hits to one hash. Both solo-only programs now
require explicit `=1` and are fixed-solo measurement doors, not load-bearing defaults. Q35-MoE
retains its code-level eager-path exclusion as defense in depth.

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_PRIME_BATCH` | 6 | max sequences per cross-request prime batch. Complete fresh prompts remain eligible for every supported batched-prime architecture. For dense models, the scheduler also batches one bounded continuation chunk per eligible cold session per tick; this prevents N cold primes from serially holding decode-ready rows. Routed-MoE carried chunks stay on per-session primes: Q35's frozen mixed c=4 cell showed both cold misses selecting EOS at 26/60 only after repeated continuation batches, while serial and one whole-fresh batch controls reached 60/60. Re-enable that class only after a realistic multi-chunk + serving-decode gate qualifies it. `=1` is the full rollback seam and restores per-session serialized cold primes. Dense Q27 retains continuation batching: the coldhol kneeraise harness moved the clean-throughput knee c12 -> c16, and the later frozen sold-shape N=5 requalification retained c16 with 825 prime-batch calls and pooled c4 hit-TTFT p50/p95 18.497/19.820 ms. Its c4 output median was 144.245 vs the old 144.462 tok/s (`-0.150%`), which the strict reducer preserves as a regression flag despite both targets remaining SELLABLE. Receipts `research/coldhol-20260812/`, `research/coldfix-20260812/`. (B=8 measured within spread of 6.) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_BATCH_MAX_T` | 2048 | max per-seq tokens eligible for batch (raised 320 -> 2048 round 35: the old crossover was measured on pre-wgmma cores; batched now wins at every tested T) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_BATCH_HOLD_MS` | 4 | batch-formation hold for staggered arrivals. The same bounded window is the post-retirement refill grace: when an interactive completion opens a client-concurrency slot while cold prefill remains, cold prefill waits briefly for the replacement request to cross the HTTP/channel boundary. On the frozen sold shape this restored pooled Q27 c4 hit-TTFT p95 from the broken 269 ms class to 19.820 ms against the 21.565 ms envelope while retaining the c16 knee. `=0` disables both holds. |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_GS` | OFF | `1` lets a fixed-solo greedy interactive session ride GraphSession replay. Replay is eager-program bit-identical, but it must degrade when a peer arrives; that would reintroduce the load-history numeric-class transition the default closes. Use only where admission guarantees the request remains solo. Round-35 history: promotion captures over an already-primed cache (`graph_session_from_cache`); the older token-wise re-prime was a 3x end-to-end loss on long prompts (6.4 -> 2.8s measured). **REACHABILITY CORRECTION (2026-08-13, hermes `a5f9431efe0a30a2` confirming an orchestrator read): this door admits RESTORED-HIT and POOL-RESUME sessions ONLY, never a cold chunked-prefill one.** The eligibility test requires `prefill_done && generated.is_empty()` at tick top, but phase (c) sweeps every `prefill_done` session into the batched decode set and emits token 1 in the SAME tick cold prefill completes — so a cold session never starts a tick in that state. The previous wording here ("promotion happens after chunked prefill") described an unreachable path. Left unwidened deliberately: `MEMRA_GS_MIN` below records the door as a net loss at every measured length post-B1FAST, so there is no perf case for making cold promotion reachable, and widening it would need a program-crossing guard (mid-request promotion is the early-EOS corruption class) |
| `MEMRA_GS_MIN` | 384 | minimum generation budget when `MEMRA_SERVE_GS=1`; inert at the default. **Do not lower on sm_120/82 SM**: after the eager B1 fast path landed, the graph door was a net loss at every measured length — N=3 `agg_tok_s` −6.93% @ 128, −3.97% @ 256, −2.60% @ 512, −1.24% @ 1024, 0/3 wins each, matching its ~340 ms capture cost. The pre-fast-path positive sweep is stale. Receipts: `research/servepath-p2-20260805/` |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_B1FAST` | OFF | `1` opts an eligible fixed-solo tick into `decode_layers_eager`, the same m=1 fusion trunk used by `decode_step_h`; unset, `0`, and other values keep B=1 on the generic batched body used at B>=2. The eager arm is not a kernel-tier fix: m=1 already used MMVQ, while the fusion chain changes FP composition. Its historical fixed-solo benefit was +8.33% q9 / +5.19% q27 decode-only at c=1 (N=5), c=8 flat; PP-N B=1 measured up to +15%. Those numbers remain valid opt-in measurements, not the correctness default. The default flipped after three independent load-history failures: Step35, Q35-MoE EOS at 15/17/25, and dense-Q27 exact 11-token EOS under controlled restored-peer arrival. `decode-batch-gate` config mode now pins default B=1 vs B=N bit identity; strict and PP arm 4 explicitly enable the eager path to retain its `decode_step_h` bit-identity coverage. Q35-MoE stays ineligible even under `=1`; gemma4/e4b, m3, and verify-exact remain excluded. Receipts: `research/servepath-p2-20260805/`, `research/b1fix-20260810/`, `research/q35bug-20260812/`, `research/eosclass-20260813/` |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_OVERLAP` | OFF / unwired | Reserved name for a future c=1 decode/readback overlap lane. The current server has no reader or scheduler wiring for this variable, so setting it has no effect. Do not describe or deploy it as default-on until the worker call, exact model/hardware/HTTP campaign, and rollback receipt land together. `PendingBatchStep` in the engine is substrate only, not a served mechanism. |
| `MEMRA_DECODE_BATCH_CAP` | auto | batched-decode chunk width door (engine assert + scheduler chunking, clamp 1..32). Unset = per-model policy: **16 on exact-16 tier models** (`decode_batch_exact16_ok` — every matmul has a bit-exact b16-class kernel; the step scopes verify_exact so the m>=16 GEMM/MMQ arms never fire), else 8. The predicate is an ALL over ~500 matmuls, so ONE class without a b16 refuses the whole checkpoint back to chunk 8 — diagnose with `MEMRA_EXACT16_WHY=1` (§4). Tier coverage widened 2026-08-06 (lane/rp-on-st) with b16 twins for NVFP4, Q8_0-base, Q4_K, Q5_K: before that every real MIXED checkpoint (NVFP4 MLP + Q4_K qkv + Q5_K gate + Q6_K output + Q8_0 ssm) was refused, GGUF and FP8-ST alike, and the Q8_0 arm was reachable only via the q8rp mirror's `_rp` twin. Inc3 receipts (5090, 9B Q8_0+mirror, `research/batched-tick-inc3-20260801/`): gate2 bit-strength PASS at chunk 12/16 (s32+s160), 32-seq engine tick +12% vs chunk 8 same-mirror (chunksweep x5). Chunk widths above the exact tier remain a MEASUREMENT DOOR: m=16 GEMM/MMQ classes bit-shift logits at step 0 (maxdiff ~1.3-2.3e-1) and m>16 has no exact kernel class at all. HISTORY: the pre-inc2 H100 probe (`research/darklane-serving-20260801/`) measured cap 15/16 flat-or-worse on the then per-seq-serial-bound tick — that verdict was re-swept after inc2's z-batched attention made the tick weight-stream-bound (the stale-verdict law) |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_DEVSAMPLE` | ON | `0` = host-sample every batched-tick row from last_logits (the pre-2026-08-01 tick). Default: greedy-no-penalty rows sample via device argmax (bit-identical to host argmax), pure-temperature rows via seeded per-row gumbel draw (distribution-equal, batch-composition-independent — decode-batch-gate gate3); top-k/top-p/min-p rows ALSO filter and draw on device (filter_stats floor + filtered gumbel, the lane/devsample-topkp extension — `devsample_meta`, worker.rs; gated by decode-batch-gate gate3d: batched-vs-isolated stream identity on mixed metas + per-draw survivor-set admission, hermes sweep 2026-08-23). Sampled penalty rows join that path through `MEMRA_SERVE_DEVPENALTY`; greedy-penalized and constrained penalty/filter compositions retain the host oracle. Kills the measured host temp-sample at 248k vocab (1.34-1.36 ms/row, ~45% of the B=8 9B tick) |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_DEVPENALTY` | OFF | `1` moves sampled requests with repetition/frequency/presence penalties onto the device while leaving ordinary device sampling unchanged. `Sampler::accept` maintains exact sparse counts over the configured sliding window; the batched epilogue uploads only unique positive-count `(token,count)` entries, applies every row's independent coefficients in one ragged CUDA launch, then runs the existing filter/Gumbel draw. Raw logits are copied before mutation and remain the only bytes parked for reuse, so a resumed request cannot double-penalize. Qwen3.8's vendor non-thinking defaults (`temperature=.7`, `top_p=.8`, `top_k=20`, `presence_penalty=1.5`) are the motivating production shape. Greedy penalties stay host-side to avoid a new argmax numeric class; constrained penalty/filter composition stays host-side pending its own gate. Qualified on RTX PRO 6000 Server Edition with `sample-check`, the causal decode-batch gate3d tooth, both-model kernel/generation/spec batteries, and interleaved N=5 serving curves: Qwen plain c1/c16 46.49→68.66 / 94.95→300.93 tok/s; Ornith plain c1/c24 79.46→176.78 / 118.77→651.77 tok/s. Naked Qwen keeps spec c1 neutral and wins after policy demotion; penalty-neutral Ornith remains structurally inert. The engine default stays off because this evidence does not qualify other GPUs or board variants. Qualification requires that deployment's own launcher smoke; an owner-authorized transfer canary may opt in sooner only with a persistent explicit rollback and without inheriting the measured performance claim. Receipts: `research/pro-device-penalty-sampling-20260824/`. |
| `MEMRA_CONSTRAIN_HOST` | off | `1` = constrained (`response_format`) sessions keep the v1 HOST mask path: full-row logits D2H + host -inf mask + host O(n_vocab) sample, no graph promotion, no spec (the merged-v1 shape; 117 vs 194 tok/s receipts `research/constrained-20260803/`). Default (constrained-full, 2026-08-03): the packed llguidance bitset H2Ds per step (~31KB) into a stable per-session device buffer and `mask_logits_f32` bans on device before the SAME device-sample/lean-logits/graph/spec paths unconstrained sessions ride (kernel-check `mask_logits_col` bit-identity arm; battery `research/constrained-full-20260803/`). Oracle/A-B seam only |
| `MEMRA_DRAFT_MASK` | ON | `0` = the drafter samples UNMASKED under a grammar (the pre-2026-08-04 shape). Default (lane/draft-mask): a constrained SPEC session clones the session's llguidance `Matcher` once per spec round, advances the clone with each proposed draft token, and bans the grammar-illegal ids in the draft head's own logits — in-graph via `mask_logits_col` on the captured draft chain (mask presence is a capture-time shape, so a parked graph of the other shape is dropped), permuted through `d2t` for trimmed FR-Spec heads. Proposals are legal by construction, so the verify-side truncation backstop stops cutting: `gram_cuts` 3/12, 3/15, 1/10, 28/30, 18/25 -> **0/N on every cell measured**. Emitted stream is unchanged by construction (verify + target sampling still decide what's emitted) — gated ON==OFF byte-identical on 7 cells, greedy and seeded-sampled, plus unconstrained 6/6 vs the pre-lane binary. Bounded tight schema: acceptance 0.561 -> 0.651, 216.6 -> 227.5 tok/s (+5.0%, N=3); clone costs 0.002 ms/round, mask 0.001 ms/position. Cells whose drafter already proposes legal tokens (json_object, loose prose) move inside noise; unconstrained is inert. Receipts `research/draft-mask-20260804/` |
| `MEMRA_BATCH_PHASE` | off | `1` = sync-bounded per-phase accumulators inside decode_step_batch (12 phases, printed by decode-batch-bench). Shares rank the tick; the added syncs inflate the total — diagnosis only |
| `MEMRA_BATCH_FA` | ON | `0` = per-seq fa_decode loop in the batched tick (the pre-inc2 launch train). Default: one blockIdx.z=sequence `fa_decode_vec_q_seqs_v4` launch + one combine per layer via the per-step K/V pointer table — per-seq bit-identical (kernel-check seqs pins; decode-batch-gate strict s160). Auto-falls back per step for non-v4 rows (t_kv<96, fp8-KV, hd!=256) or an `fa_split_keys` rung straddle inside the batch (batched-tick inc2, 2026-08-01: B=8 engine +19.7%) |
| `MEMRA_BATCH_APPEND` | ON | `0` = per-seq KV-append loop in the batched tick. Default: one z-batched `append_quantize_kv_q8_0_q5_1_seqs` launch per layer through the same pointer table — cache bytes bit-identical (kernel-check pin). fp8-KV rides the per-seq g-module path |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_LEANLOGITS` | ON | `0` = full [B, n_vocab] logits D2H every batched tick (the pre-inc2 tick). Default: device-sampled rows skip the D2H; the last row parks on-device per cache (`Cache.last_logits_dev`) and is D2H'd once at retire for the KV-reuse pool (the audited last_logits consumer). decode-batch-gate gate3c pins lean==full bitwise. Serving: 9B temp-0.7 c=8/16/32 medians 654/657/659 tok/s vs 565/519/508 lean-off (H100, N=4, `research/batched-tick-inc2-20260801/`) |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_DEDUP` | ON | `0` restores independent cold primes for same-window fanout (rollback/A-B seam). Default: interactive cold requests with the same exact `(model, tenant-scoped cache namespace, >=64-token prefix)` prime one budget-capped common prefix; siblings deep-copy the resulting snapshot, receive exact cached-token credit, and hold one entry lease each until retirement. Token similarity is never evaluated across tenant/salt boundaries. Step35 suffixes keep their per-request continuation-prime path because its fresh PP batch core deliberately refuses carried caches. |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_MB` | derived: 2 entries, boot-clamped | CROSS-REQUEST PREFIX CACHE byte budget (0 = off). With no override, memra derives two full-`MEMRA_CTX` snapshots of the largest loaded model: the sum of every trunk full-attention layer's K+V bytes per token times context, plus fixed trunk recurrent state. MTP/NextN head blocks are excluded because prefix snapshots retain only trunk state. It clamps that request to CUDA driver free VRAM measured after model load minus the 1.5 GiB serving-transient reserve. A valid explicit value remains authoritative and unclamped, including `0`; the value is binary MiB. Token-prefix-keyed compact device snapshots share one worker-global byte budget; `(model, cache namespace)` partitions lookup visibility only. Eviction is byte-SLRU by default, while `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_POLICY=lru` restores plain global LRU. A NEW session sharing a >=64-token prompt prefix with cached traffic skips that prefill. `prefix_cache_skips_budget` counts entries too large ever to fit, while `prefix_cache_skips_pinned` counts temporary refusal beside live leases; both are operator-only `/metrics` fields and the first whole-budget refusal is a loud server warning. Dual-PP intersection (2026-08-12, hermes 4beb2984): the naked dual-active placement and prefix snapshots are validated together on the box1 PRO pair — byte identity 3/3, hit TTFT 1.3ms, 0 slot collisions (`research/prefixmoney-20260812/raw/box1-20260812T005749Z`). Ring-ON (`MEMRA_SWA_RING=1`) and host-bounce placements still refuse/disable snapshots. Cold prompts seed full-prompt entries at prefill-done; additionally, when the LCP taught nothing, a plain chat-turns request parks a grid-aligned snapshot at the end of the FIRST message (the render-stable system prompt — `first_message_boundary`, worker.rs, lane/moebatch-q35moe 2026-08-21; shapes with tools/think/tool turns return no boundary and keep the full-prompt seed only). With `MEMRA_PREFIX_PARTIAL_RESTORE=1`, a transformer-only miss with a long LCP copies the position-addressed K/V rows through that split and primes only the suffix, so request 2 reuses it. Hybrid conv/SSM and routed-MoE mid-entry splits refuse observably and retain the cold-prime/snapshot boundary-learning path (request 3+ hits); exact whole-entry boundaries remain reusable. Sessions win over unpinned cache residency (allocation failure evicts every unpinned entry and retries), while hit/fanout leases protect their entry until retirement. Entries bind the full `(model, cache namespace)` identity, internal layout version, per-plane row strides, and bounded source sizes before any restore copy. Cached-vs-fresh exactness is gated 16/16 partial + 16/16 full (`research/prompt-cache-20260802/`). Usage exposes `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`. Entries are isolated by the request's optional `cache_salt`; no salt preserves the `""` single-tenant namespace (`docs/SERVING.md` "Per-tenant isolation"). |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_POLICY` | `slru` | Prefix-cache eviction policy. `slru` (default) = byte-budgeted probation/protected with promotion on first reuse (`research/slrucache-20260813/`: hot-set reuse 107/120 -> 115/120, hot thrash 13 -> 5, post-promotion hot thrash exactly 0, all hits byte-identical). `lru` = plain global LRU: `capacity_victim` evicts the GLOBAL oldest entry across both segments. **Fixed 2026-08-13** — until then this seam was a near-no-op that did the opposite of its purpose: it only forced `protected_pct=100`, and since there was no policy branch in `capacity_victim` (probation-only) while entries still entered probation and still promoted on reuse, a 100% protected share merely set `protected_target_bytes = budget` so `rebalance_protected` could never demote — every entry that earned one hit became permanently unevictable and each newcomer was selected as its own victim. An operator reaching for this flag during a hit-rate collapse therefore deepened it. Regression test: `prefix_cache_lru_policy_evicts_the_global_oldest_not_only_probation` (verified RED against the pre-fix behavior). **The rollback exists because SLRU is NOT universally better:** `research/slrutarget-20260813/` found a real losing shape — after a complete hot-cohort turnover a stale protected cohort traps the new cyclic cohort in probation and the hit rate goes to **0% where plain LRU reaches 75%**, reproduced at both 4,096 and 49,152 MiB and for Q27-only, Q35-only, and worker-global paired entries. Note `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_PROTECTED_PCT` is NOT an LRU selector (it only sizes the protected share, 1..99); this flag is. Until live telemetry shows which shape our traffic has, an operator whose cache hits collapse needs one flag to get LRU back. |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_PROTECTED_PCT` | 80 | Prefix-cache SLRU protected share by resident bytes; integer 1..99, with malformed/out-of-range values falling back to 80. New entries enter probation and promote only on reuse. Protected overflow demotes protected LRU; capacity pressure evicts probation LRU first. Probation may borrow unused protected bytes, so the total `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_MB` budget remains usable and individually fitting large entries are still admitted. A pinned fanout snapshot that cannot fit without crossing the protected share is served from its private session copies but not retained. The byte ceiling, oversized-entry refusal, tenant `cache_salt` visibility, live pins, and emergency flush semantics are unchanged. |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_PARTIAL_RESTORE` | **OFF (default) since 2026-08-13** | `=1` arms immediate LCP-boundary partial restore FOR MEASUREMENT ONLY. **The lane that built this mechanism returned NO-GO** (`research/lcprestore-20260813/RESULTS.md`, merged 6249b0096): source->restored K/V state SHA-256 MATCH at splits 64/512/2048/4374, yet generated output BYTE-DIVERGES from a genuinely-cold request at splits **512 and 2048** (verbatim BYTE MISMATCH reducer lines committed), and `research/splitiso-20260813/` (verdict **BOUNDARY-IDENTIFIED**) then named the cause: it is not alignment, tiling, page, or split position, but the worker's own numerical-program fork `eager_mono && carried` — a cold Gemma request runs ONE monolithic `gemma4_prime` while a partial-prefix hit must run every suffix token through T=1 `decode_step`. Prime attends over transient pre-quantized/bf16 Q/K/V operands that are NOT part of the prefix-cache object, so matching K/V with mismatched output is expected: restored rows are the complete *cache* state but not the complete *decode program*. Divergence appears wherever that always-present perturbation crosses a greedy argmax, which is why failures are content-sensitive islands rather than a geometric threshold. The mechanism shipped default-ON in that merge by mistake and was flipped OFF the next day (hermes `e993b2839ea565f2`); it stays off until a suffix-prime path exists that is bit-identical to the cold program (the legal shapes are: refuse partial restore, or re-prime the suffix through the SAME monolithic program cold requests use — never a mid-request program switch). This is a DISTINCT defect from the Q27 dense restored-hit 11/60-token EOS (`research/cachesize-20260813/raw/attempt7-cache-hit-eos/`): `research/splitiso-20260813/RESULTS.md:206-212` states explicitly that it does not close that class. Both are instances of CLAUDE.md §One numeric program per request. When armed, it applies only to transformer-only entries with a non-empty suffix; hybrid recurrent and routed-MoE mid-entry splits refuse. |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_SPLIT_TRACE` | off | `1` D2H-hashes every logical K/V byte plus position/len mirrors at a cold split snapshot, partial source, and restored destination. Exactness-gate diagnostics only; it synchronizes and is intentionally expensive. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_SAMPLED` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-19** | `0` = SAMPLED prefix-cache hits serve on the PLAIN path (the v0.93.0 posture), leaving greedy restores untouched — the one-flip fleet rollback for lane/sampled-hit-spec. Default ON: a whole-entry hit re-arms a warm spec session for sampled requests too. WHY THIS IS THE DEFAULT: v0.93.0 shipped the restore greedy-only and the DE deploy window then measured 3 cache hits / 3 plain downgrades / **0 restores** on a customer tenant, because API traffic is sampled by default (`temperature` 1.0, `main.rs default_temperature`) — the headline feature was inert for exactly the traffic that pays. The greedy-only premise ("a sampled hit's first token must be host-sampled from the boundary logits") did not survive the code it protected: at the time the COLD sampled spec burst did not host-sample its own first token either — it argmaxed `prime_logits`, as did every continuation burst (`MEMRA_SPEC_SAMPLED_BOUNDARY` fixed that on 2026-08-19; both paths now DRAW that token from the same row at Philox counter 0, so the identity below is preserved by construction instead of by both sides being greedy). Sampled spec is DISTRIBUTIONALLY exact, not byte-equal to plain sampling (`MEMRA_SPEC_TEMP` row). So the refusal preserved no property; it only split one request shape across two sampling programs (cold: spec, cached: plain). The restored session's seed rule and Philox counters ((0,0) at admit, as in any fresh session) are bit-identical to a cold session's, which is what the gate measures: per seed, the full-cover sampled hit's bytes AND its accepted/drafted counts equal the cold sampled leader's. The penalized-sampled refusal this row originally carried was **LIFTED 2026-08-19** (lane/sampled-spec-quality): the burst's penalty window now spans the session (`MEMRA_SPEC_PEN_SESSION`), and a restored session's `committed` is the whole prompt, so a penalized restore reproduces the cold leader exactly like every other sampled cell. It returns, named, under `MEMRA_SPEC_PEN_SESSION=0`. Gate: `tools/spec-on-cache-hit-gate.sh qwen …` sampled cells, plus its `MEMRA_HITGATE_TEETH=1` arm which asserts THIS door holds sampled hits plain. Receipts: darklanes `research/spec-cache-20260818/SAMPLED-HIT.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_SAMPLED_BOUNDARY` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-19** | `0` = a sampled spec burst emits an ARGMAX at its own boundary (the pre-2026-08-19 posture); greedy is byte-unaffected either way. WHAT A BOUNDARY TOKEN IS: a burst's FIRST emitted token does not come from the accept walk — it comes off a logits row that already exists (the prime's last row on a cold burst, the row after the last committed token on a continuation burst, the prefix-cache entry's boundary row on a converted hit). All three were `argmax` in BOTH sampling regimes, so a sampled stream took a greedy token once per burst — measured, not estimated, on 18 real agent prompts at the API's own default temperature (1.0), 256 tokens each: **3.52% of generated tokens were boundary tokens at c=1 (one per 28.4 tokens — below the 1-in-32 the burst size suggests, because bursts also end on EOS, budget and admission yield), and 48.8% of those draws returned a DIFFERENT token than the argmax they replaced ⇒ 1.71% of a customer's tokens came from the wrong distribution.** At c=8 the rate falls to 0.50% for a reason worth knowing: the spec admit/demote gate (`MEMRA_SPEC_GATE`) keeps only 2 of 18 requests on the spec path under load, so the exposure is CONCENTRATED in the low-concurrency interactive tier rather than diluted by it. Customer-visible corollary: at temperature > 0 every request's FIRST token was the greedy token, identical for every seed. Default ON draws the boundary token from the request's own filtered/penalized target using the SESSION's Philox stream (never a second stream — that would be a fresh distributional bug), which is also what keeps a restored session's seed bit-identical to the cold session's. Oracles: `sample_check` arm 9 (20k draws, TV against the CPU-referenced target with the Monte-Carlo floor printed, plus teeth cells that FAIL the argmax rule and an unpenalized target), and `tools/spec-on-cache-hit-gate.sh` (all three sites fired, deviation observed, per-seed hit==cold identity intact). Receipts: darklanes `research/spec-cache-20260818/SAMPLED-QUALITY.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PEN_SESSION` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-19** | `0` = each spec burst restarts the penalty window from the `prompt` slice it is handed (the pre-2026-08-19 posture). That is the defect: a continuation burst is handed NO prompt, so `repetition_penalty` / `frequency_penalty` / `presence_penalty` silently reset at every burst boundary and saw an empty window for the majority of a sampled stream's tokens — i.e. penalties did not do what the API contract says over a long stream. Default ON seeds the window from `committed ++ prompt` (the last `max(penalty_last_n, 64)` tokens), which is what the plain sampler's own history does, and additionally counts the burst's boundary token (pre-lane it was never recorded at all). Byte-identical to the old posture for a cold turn-1 burst at the default window, so the change is confined to continuation bursts and restored sessions. This door also gates the penalized-sampled prefix-cache restore: with it shut, `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_SAMPLED`'s penalized refusal comes back and names this flag. Receipts: darklanes `research/spec-cache-20260818/SAMPLED-QUALITY.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_RESUME_SAMPLER` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-20** | `0` = whole-session spec resume goes back to the pre-lane probe that compared prompts and never samplers (`SamplerIdentity::legacy_admits`) — the rollback door AND the within-binary A/B arm for the refusal's cost. Default ON: a parked whole session's committed state is sampler-shaped in more places than the draft graph (penalty window, Philox stream position, draft plane), so a resume whose request sampler differs from the identity that shaped the parked session is REFUSED AND NAMED (`spec-reuse REFUSED: parked session's sampler differs on <field>`), on all three probe tiers; admitted resumes are tagged `[sampler=equivalent]` vs `[sampler=unchecked]`. `seed` is carried but deliberately NOT compared: the only parked state that bakes it (the sampled draft graph) is already re-keyed on it (`SampledGraphKey`), and an omitted seed draws fresh per-request entropy, so comparing it would refuse every seed-omitting sampled conversation for zero soundness gain (pinned by `seed_alone_does_not_refuse` / `seed_alone_still_rekeys_the_draft_graph`). This is a POLICY door, not an exactness door — with it shut, `SampledGraphKey` still refuses the cross-regime graph launch. `/metrics`: `spec_pool_sampler_refusals`. Receipts: darklanes `research/spec-cache-20260818/SESSION-RESUME-PREDICATE.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_LOAD_GUARD` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-19** | `0` = a sampled prefix-cache restore re-arms spec regardless of load (the un-guarded lane/sampled-hit-spec posture). Default ON admits a sampled restore only when the request is **SOLO** — nothing else in flight — and refuses otherwise, **named**: `[spec-restore-guard] sampled restore REFUSED: demand N > SOLO watermark 1 (band LOW=l HIGH=h; projected active=a, queued=q, http in-flight i)`. WHY, measured on box1 (2 x RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96GB, 27B NVFP4+MTP, sold shape = 4 860-token shared prefix, interleaved passes): the lever is **1.623x** solo at 192 output tokens (115.44 vs 71.12 tok/s, engaged 5/5 vs 0/5, `cached_tokens=4860` both arms so the delta is decode-only) and **1.350x** at c1 on the 60-token sold row — but **0.669x at c2** and **0.813x at c16**. The crossover is between c1 and c2: two concurrent sampled spec sessions already cost a third of aggregate throughput, because phase (a) steps each burst in a serial host loop and phase (c) excludes spec sessions from batched decode, so two of them serialise what would otherwise be a 2-wide batch. MECHANISM, and why the guard is sampled-only: admission (`choose_spec_k`, K=0 above LOW) and the tick demotion sweep (`spec_gate_high`) both decide 'busy' on the MTP band, and **sampled sessions are excluded from the demotion sweep** (the handoff would move generation to a batched row with its own sampler history and Philox stream, an unmeasured distributional seam). So a sampled restore admitted while the box was quiet rides the serial spec queue until the request ends, and nothing can clean up after it; a GREEDY restore in the same position IS handed back the moment load arrives, measures 1.00x at c16, and is left untouched. The watermark is SOLO rather than the band's LOW=2 because LOW=2 was measured on a 5090 with a short greedy shape (c2 1.08x WIN there) and admits exactly the c2 shape that loses hardest here — the same SOLO rule `gspec_k` and sampled/non-demotable dspark apply. The band is kept as a CEILING (`min(LOW, 1)`), so a PP-2 placement that admits no spec admits no restores. DEMAND is `max(worker-visible, HTTP in-flight)` read as LATE as possible (at the refusal site, after the restore's own 312 MB KV copy): reading `active + queue` at tick top leaves the HEAD of an arriving fan-out unrefusable, and one engaged row is worth ~9.6% of a c16 wave's aggregate. An operator `MEMRA_SPEC_K` pin or `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE=0` owns the whole policy and bypasses the guard. Gate: `tools/spec-cache-gate.sh` with `MEMRA_CACHEGATE_TEMP>0` — its sampled verdict derives the watermark from the server's own boot line, requires the refusal line above it, and requires the 1.05x recovery at/below it. Receipts: darklanes `research/spec-cache-20260818/SAMPLED-LOAD-GUARD.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_REPUBLISH` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-19** | `0` = a spec session restored from a prefix-cache hit publishes nothing (the pre-2026-08-19 posture). Default ON: after the restore feeds its prompt suffix, the session captures its OWN prompt-end boundary and the worker's existing publication sweep publishes it, so the next turn of a growing conversation hits a STRICTLY LONGER prefix. Pre-lane, publication was armed for cold sessions only (`spec_resumed == 0`) and both engine capture sites require a non-continuation burst, so a namespace learned exactly ONE boundary and turn 3+ could never advance it — multi-turn spec paid a constant, not a growing, share of prefill. WHOLE-ENTRY ONLY: the captured boundary is the restored session's own prompt end, so the republished entry satisfies `entry_pos == fed_len` and the rolled-back mid-entry partial-restore class stays closed. Costs one `Cache::snapshot` per restored session with a suffix (GDN conv/ssm copy) plus one cache entry, and a failed snapshot is silent (publication is an optimization). Gate: the growth cells of `tools/spec-on-cache-hit-gate.sh` (turn 3's cached prefix > turn 2's, byte-identical to the spec-off twin, and the teeth arm asserts the pre-lane stall). Receipts: darklanes `research/spec-cache-20260818/SAMPLED-QUALITY.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_STABLE_BOUNDARY` | **ON (default) since 2026-08-21** | `0` = the spec tier's turn checkpoint and restored-session republication go back to PROMPT-END (the pre-lane posture) — the rollback seam and the toothed multi-turn gate's broken arm. Default ON ports the plain tier's 2026-08-09 stable pre-generation boundary law (`plain_checkpoint_boundary`: checkpoint BEFORE the last turn-marker run) to the spec tier, in three places: (1) `turn_ckpt` is captured at the stable boundary — a prompt-end spec checkpoint includes the template's live assistant-generation header (`<|im_start|>assistant\n<think>\n`), which every re-rendering client replaces, so `affinity_match` diverged ~2 tokens BELOW the checkpoint and spec affinity declined 100% of multi-turn agent traffic (measured: `spec-affinity: declined (history diverged at 6811 of checkpoint 6813)`); (2) WARM bursts (affinity-rewound / pool-resumed sessions priming their own delta) also stop at the new turn's stable boundary and refresh `turn_ckpt` there, mirroring the resumed plain session's prefill tick; (3) a restored session's extended-entry republication (`MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_REPUBLISH`) captures at the stable boundary instead of prompt-end AND installs a live `turn_ckpt`, so on a hybrid (whole-entry restores only) the published entry can actually serve the next re-rendered turn — pre-lane every republished entry's poisoned 2-token tail made the hit boundary FREEZE at the first lcp-split entry forever (measured: cached 6811 of 38,228 prompt tokens at turn 8 = 1.5% by turn 7 vs 98.6% on the plain arm; TTFT 10–13.6 s vs 0.36–4.5 s; spec LOSES 2.8–3.0× wall from turn ~3). Costs one extra `Cache::snapshot` per turn (GDN conv/ssm copy — the plain tier already pays it). Gate: the darklanes multi-turn gate (`research/multiturn-cache-20260821/`, cached_tokens must grow per turn + TTFT stays in the plain-with-cache class; the `=0` arm must FAIL with the frozen boundary named). Receipts: darklanes `research/multiturn-cache-20260821/FRSPEC-FIX-20260821.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_ANATOMY` | off | `1` = eager draft-step phase timers (glue/attn/ffn/head, sync-bounded, one cumulative print every 128 steps) — the diagnostic seam the draftcost lane profiled with. Adds phase-boundary syncs while on; diagnostics only, never a serving default |
| `MEMRA_STATE_COPY_BATCH` | on | batch the dspark round's GDN state snap/commit copies into indirect-table launches (engine-bundle slice 1; the 192+112 per-round DtoD copies collapse to a handful) and the slice-3 slab commit's two-copy restore; `0` = per-layer view copies (the pre-bundle arm, kept as the rollback seam — byte-identity between the arms is gated by the dspark E2E cells) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_BOUNDARY_TRACE` | off | `1` names every sampled boundary draw on stderr — `[spec-boundary] site=<cold-prime\|burst-tail-commit\|burst-tail-nopending\|flush-pending\|restore-suffix-feed\|restore-full-cover> sampled=<id> argmax=<id> deviates=<0\|1>`. Diagnostics only (no behaviour change); it is how the boundary RATE and the deviation rate are measured rather than estimated, and the hit gate asserts on it. Costs one extra device argmax per boundary when on. |

### Tuning / diagnostic seams (sm_90a lane)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_CUDA_ARCH` | 120a | build arch: `90a` = the Hopper lane (wgmma/TMA kernels compile; portable-PTX correctness path rides along) |
| `MEMRA_FA_SPLIT` | ctx-adaptive ladder | fixed FA-decode split size; ladder re-swept on 132-SM H100 round 35 — holds (sp32 +0.35% = noise) |

### Refuted on this lane (mechanism in ARCHITECTURE-H100.md; no flag shipped)

Monolithic + piecewise prime CUDA graphs (Lt address-variant kernels); W8A8/fp8/CUTLASS/
Lt-autotune prefill GEMMs at m=512 AND m=2048; FA3 shapes v6-v9; 12 k45 scheduling forms;
K3 solve ILP-split AND tf32 inverse-product; v6 exchange-free k45; Q8_0-EXACT int8 GEMM
(triple-refuted: per-block rescale 5.4x naive, 17x pipelined — ptxas C7517 serializes
cross-bank GMMA register reads); FA3-TMA-ring k45 rebuild (refuted by composition).
The remaining single-seq prefill residual (~27% vs vLLM's INT8 GEMM class) is an
owner-gated accuracy decision (w8a8-class numerics change model outputs).

## Removed in the 2026-07-08 flag audit (concluded flags — JSONL rows are the record)

`MEMRA_SPEC_DSPARK` (lived hours, 2026-07-30): DSpark-class marginal-rate verify window
(arXiv 2607.05147) — S_{j+1}·T(j) > E[tok](j)·t_draft with profiled t_draft/t_verify EMAs.
Measured FLAT: never cuts at accept ≥ 0.8 (31B depth: identical rounds/accepts, −0.4%
noise), par on the 26B depth high mode (278 vs 281, −1.2%); the fixed 0.7 self-keyed cut
already covers the low-accept case. Arm deleted; note: the survival estimator used the
current-conditional-as-proxy — a learned estimator (the paper) could cut smarter.
